## Description

Two detection-only modules for CVE-2026-93952, a critical improper input validation flaw (CWE-20) in on-premises Arista (formerly VMware) VeloCloud Orchestrator (VCO), disclosed 2026-09-22 and reported as actively exploited (added to the CISA KEV catalog). Arista PSIRT scores it CVSS v3.1 10.0 / CVSS v4.0 9.5. Neither module attempts exploitation; this covers detection and compromise-triage only.

1. `auxiliary/scanner/http/velocloud_orchestrator_version` confirms the target is a VCO - using the Shodan/nuclei-compatible login-favicon MurmurHash3 (`-2062596654`) as the authoritative product signal, with a landing-page string match as fallback - then makes a best-effort read of the version, parses it with `Rex::Version`, and reports whether it falls within a CVE-2026-93952 affected range per release train:

    | Train | Affected | Fixed |
    | ----- | -------- | ----- |
    | 5.2 | `<= 5.2.3.15` | `5.2.3.16` |
    | 6.4 | `<= 6.4.2.7` | `6.4.2.8` |
    | 6.1 | `<= 6.1.3.7` | fix pending |
    | 7.0 | `<= 7.0.0.2` | fix pending |

    The output states that actual exposure also requires certificate-based Edge-to-VCO authentication (Certificate Acquire or Certificate Required mode), which can't be determined by remote version fingerprinting. A "vulnerable" result means "running an affected version," not "confirmed exploitable."

2. `post/linux/gather/velocloud_cve_2026_93952_ioc_check` is a read-only forensic check for an operator to run on a VCO host they administer. It looks for the published host-side indicators of compromise: known malicious file artifacts, the MD5 of the `vc-sysmond` implant, the malicious `vc-sysmon.service` systemd unit, and the reported attacker IPs in nginx access logs. It reports a vuln and note on any match and is explicit that a clean result is not proof the host is uncompromised.

Both modules ship with documentation pages under `documentation/modules/`.

Related Issue: tracking issue #21940

## Breaking Changes

None. Two new modules and their docs; nothing existing is modified.

## Reviewer Notes

**Product detection is implemented; version detection is the open question.** The favicon MurmurHash3 (`-2062596654`, per the ProjectDiscovery favicon-hash template) is the only confirmed *unauthenticated* fingerprint for the product, and it is now wired in as an additive signal. The hash machinery is validated offline: the MurmurHash3 x86_32 implementation matches the canonical Python `mmh3` vectors, and the 76-character base64 wrap reproduces Python `base64.encodebytes` byte-for-byte (Ruby's `Base64.encode64` wraps at 60 and would produce a different value). The signal is additive, so detection still succeeds on either the favicon hash or the string match - an incorrect constant only falls back to string matching, with no regression.

What the favicon hash does **not** give is the version. Public research (the Arista VeloCloud OpenAPI guide, the vcoclient project, ProjectDiscovery nuclei templates) indicates the VCO REST API under `/portal/rest` is authenticated, and no public unauthenticated version endpoint is documented, so an unauthenticated version read may not be feasible on every build. That is what the live-instance validation needs to answer, and it is called out in the code comments. The favicon *constant* itself is nuclei-sourced and, like the version read, still wants confirmation against a real VCO. Feedback from anyone with on-prem VCO access on how the build version is exposed would be welcome.

Scope: the exploitation primitive for CVE-2026-93952 is intentionally out of scope. The vendor advisory withholds the mechanics, two release trains remain unpatched, and the flaw is under active exploitation. This is detection and triage only.

## Verification Steps

- [x] `bundle exec rspec` on both specs -> **37 examples, 0 failures**.
- [x] rubocop clean on both modules and both specs.
- [x] msftidy clean on both modules.
- [ ] Pending, and the reason this is a draft: `get_version` (and the favicon constant) validated against a live VCO instance. The version-range comparison and the hash machinery are spec-verified; the *values* they compare against (the build-version signal and the favicon hash) are best guesses until confirmed on a real orchestrator.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->57:T198c,## Description

This adds an exploit module for CVE-2026-87902, an unauthenticated local file
inclusion in WordPress core page-template resolution.

WordPress builds the page template hierarchy from the `pagename` query
variable. `get_page_template()` appends `page-{urldecode(pagename)}.php` to the
template candidates and `locate_template()` requires the resolved file without
verifying that it stays inside the active theme. Percent-encoded traversal
sequences survive `sanitize_title_for_query()` and are decoded during template
resolution, so an unauthenticated attacker can include a readable `.php` file
outside the active theme.

The module confirms the inclusion with read-only requests and, when the target
meets the PEAR pre-conditions (`pearcmd.php` reachable and `register_argc_argv`
On), escalates to remote code execution. The query string becomes pearcmd.php
argv, `pear config-create` writes a small PHP stub, and the same inclusion
primitive executes it. The stub deletes itself before running the payload, so
no artifact is left on disk.

Affected releases and pre-conditions are documented in the module and in
`documentation/modules/exploit/unix/webapp/wp_page_template_lfi_rce.md`.

**Related Issue:** None

## Breaking Changes

None

## Reviewer Notes

- `check` only returns `Vulnerable` with hard evidence: it includes a file
  outside the theme and compares the response with the normal page render.
- If the PEAR pre-conditions are missing, `exploit` fails with a clear message
  and does not write anything.
- Tested with the `cmd/unix/reverse_bash` and `cmd/unix/generic` payloads.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->58:T1159,## Description

Follow-up to #21725 (merged). This addresses @dledda-r7's review comment on that PR, which @jheysel-r7 asked me to handle in a separate PR.

`auxiliary/spoof/ipv6/ipv6_ra_dns_takeover` advertises the attacker as the victim's recursive DNS server by sending an RDNSS option (RFC 8106) inside Router Advertisements. That option ships with a lifetime of `0xFFFFFFFF`, which RFC 8106 section 5.1 defines as "use this RDNSS indefinitely." Before this change, `cleanup` stopped the advertiser thread and closed the pcap handle but never told clients to stop using the rogue resolver. A client that had adopted the entry would keep pointing at the attacker address after the module stopped, which can leave name resolution broken on that host until it happens to receive a corrective Router Advertisement or reboots.

This change makes `cleanup` multicast a final Router Advertisement that sets the RDNSS lifetime (and the router lifetime) to `0`. Per RFC 8106 section 5.1 a lifetime of `0` instructs clients to stop using that RDNSS entry, so teardown now withdraws the rogue resolver instead of leaving it pinned for an effectively infinite lifetime.

This is best-effort teardown by nature: `cleanup` does not run on a hard kill or a crash, so the withdrawal is a courtesy rather than a guarantee. It is still strictly better than leaving the entry pinned at an effectively infinite lifetime, which is the situation the review comment raised.

**Related Issue:** Follow-up to #21725

## Breaking Changes

None.

## Reviewer Notes

Where to look and why:

- `cleanup` now stops the advertiser thread first, then calls a new `send_ra_withdrawal` before closing the pcap handle. The ordering matters so the withdrawal is the only thing touching the pcap handle at teardown.
- `send_ra_withdrawal` multicasts the lifetime-`0` Router Advertisement a few times, because Router Advertisements are unacknowledged multicast and can be dropped. It is wrapped so a failure during teardown is logged rather than raised.
- `build_ra_dns_packet` gained `router_lifetime:` and `dns_lifetime:` keyword arguments, defaulting to the advertising values, so a single builder produces both the advertisement and the withdrawal.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/modules/auxiliary/spoof/ipv6/ipv6_ra_dns_takeover_spec.rb` -> 20 examples, 0 failures.
2. - [x] `ruby tools/dev/msftidy.rb modules/auxiliary/spoof/ipv6/ipv6_ra_dns_takeover.rb` -> clean.

## Test Evidence

New `#cleanup` specs read the RDNSS lifetime back out of the injected Router Advertisement and assert it is `0`, and assert no withdrawal is injected when the pcap handle was never opened.

```
$ bundle exec rspec spec/modules/auxiliary/spoof/ipv6/ipv6_ra_dns_takeover_spec.rb
20 examples, 0 failures

$ ruby tools/dev/msftidy.rb modules/auxiliary/spoof/ipv6/ipv6_ra_dns_takeover.rb
1 file inspected, no offenses detected
```

### On-wire lab test

Ran the module against a live IPv6 segment and captured the traffic to confirm the withdrawal is actually emitted on cleanup.

Setup: attacker on a shared layer-2 segment, a Windows Server 2022 host on the same segment, and `tcpdump` capturing ICMPv6 for the full run. The module advertised `SPOOF_IP6` (`dead:beef::53` in the test) as the RDNSS every 5 seconds; the job was then killed to trigger `cleanup`. The captured Router Advertisements were decoded with `tshark` to read the RDNSS option lifetime.

| phase | when | RDNSS server | RDNSS lifetime |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| advertise | during the run, every 5s | `dead:beef::53` | `4294967295` (`0xffffffff`, infinite) |
| withdrawal | at teardown | `dead:beef::53` | `0` |

The three withdrawal Router Advertisements carrying lifetime `0` were emitted by `cleanup` immediately after the infinite-lifetime advertisements stopped, which is exactly the behavior this change adds.

Scope of the test: the capture proves the withdrawal is sent on the wire as designed. I was not able to demonstrate the full client-side adopt-then-drop on the host console in this lab, because the Windows Server 2022 host available to me does not adopt an RA-learned RDNSS entry from a DNS-only advertisement (router lifetime `0`, the module's stealthy default) in its configuration. That is a property of the test host rather than of this change; the withdrawal packet itself is correct and RFC 8106 compliant regardless of whether a given client had adopted the entry.59:T1104,## Description

- `.github/workflows/lint.yml`: `msftidy_docs` is its own job (`Lint msftidy_docs`), so a failure is clearly a docs failure rather than a module one. The existing `msftidy` job now only covers modules.
- `tools/dev/pre-commit-hook.rb`: adds an `MSFTIDY_TARGET` env var (`modules`, `docs`, or unset for both, which keeps local hook behavior unchanged). Docs are checked only when newly added (`--diff-filter=A`), not across the whole `documentation/` tree.

Tested the hook locally on a simulated merge commit: a broken new doc is flagged and the hook exits non-zero, an edited existing doc is ignored, a compliant new doc passes, and `MSFTIDY_TARGET=modules` skips the docs check.5b:T147a,## Description

Over RPC, `module.execute('exploit', ...)` and `module.execute('evasion', ...)` return a
success-shaped `{ "job_id" => nil, "uuid" => nil }` (HTTP 200, no `error` key) when the module's
options fail to validate. No job starts, and on the exploit path the reason reaches no structured
channel, so the client sees a success response for a run that never started. The `auxiliary`/`post` branches of the
same `rpc_execute` already raise a structured `Msf::RPC::Exception` for this identical failure.

The wrappers (`exploit_simple`, `Evasion.run_simple`) rescue the failure for the console and
return without starting a job, and the RPC helper builds its response from the resulting nil
`job_id` without checking. This PR:

1. Propagates the error to the original module instance on the wrappers' failure path (mirroring
   the `job_id` propagation already done on success), so the RPC layer can report which option failed.
2. Raises a structured error when `mod.job_id` is nil after the wrapper returns, instead of a
   success-shaped body. `job_id` is a reliable signal here: RPC always runs with `RunAsJob => true`
   (a successful launch gets a non-nil id) on a freshly created instance (`job_id` starts nil).

The console rescue is unchanged; only the RPC caller stops discarding the outcome. This matches the
return-contract guidance in `AGENTS.md`.

**Related Issue:** Fixes #21927

## Breaking Changes

The `exploit`/`evasion` failure path now raises `Msf::RPC::Exception` (500) instead of returning
200 with `{job_id: nil}`. `auxiliary`/`post` already behave this way, so a client that handles a
failed `module.execute` for those types already handles this shape. Happy to gate or deprecate
instead if you prefer.

## Reviewer Notes

- Key change: `_error_unless_started(mod)` in `rpc_module.rb` plus its call sites in
  `_run_exploit`/`_run_evasion`; the propagation is two lines in each wrapper's existing
  `OptionValidateError` rescue.
- Out of scope: a failure *after* the job starts (surfaces via `module.results`), and touching the
  legacy `rescue ::Exception` arms.

## Verification Steps

     ```

     ```
2. - [ ] On unpatched `master` (restore the two `lib/` files, keep the specs), the same specs fail
     ("but nothing was raised" / error recorded 0 times), proving they exercise the fix.

</details>5c:T1e09,## Description

This adds an exploit module for [CVE-2026-33439](https://github.com/OpenIdentityPlatform/OpenAM/security/advisories/GHSA-2cqq-rpvq-g5qj), an unauthenticated Java deserialization vulnerability affecting OpenIdentityPlatform OpenAM through version 16.0.5.

OpenAM JATO ViewBean endpoints deserialize the attacker-controlled `jato.clientSession` HTTP parameter without validation. The module sends a Java serialization gadget chain using OpenAM-bundled Apache Click and Xalan classes:

`java.util.PriorityQueue` → Apache Click `ColumnComparator` → Xalan `TemplatesImpl`

The command is inserted into the embedded translet class before the serialized object is Base64URL-encoded and sent to the vulnerable endpoint. No external gadget-generation tooling, such as ysoserial, is required at runtime.

The module provides two targets:

- A Unix command target for direct command execution.
- A Linux x64 dropper target that stages a Meterpreter payload using `CmdStager`.

The `check` method identifies an accessible OpenAM password-reset JATO form by looking for the expected OpenAM, `tfUserAttr`, and `jato.pageSession` markers. It does not fingerprint the installed OpenAM version.

Related references:

- https://github.com/OpenIdentityPlatform/OpenAM/security/advisories/GHSA-2cqq-rpvq-g5qj
- https://github.com/projectdiscovery/nuclei-templates/blob/main/http/cves/2026/CVE-2026-33439.yaml

## Related Issue

None — no existing `suggestion-module` issue was found for this CVE.

## Breaking Changes

None

## Setup

A vulnerable instance can be stood up with the vendor's official Docker image, pinned to the affected version:

```text
docker run -h openam-01.domain.com -p 8080:8080 --name openam-01 openidentityplatform/openam:16.0.5
```

Give it a few minutes to finish first-boot initialization, then browse to `http://openam-01.domain.com:8080/openam` to confirm it's up (map that hostname to the Docker host's address in `/etc/hosts`, use `--add-host`, or simply use `http://localhost:8080/openam` when running against the local Docker host). The container's hostname must match how you reach it, since OpenAM bakes it into the URLs it generates internally.

**You must complete OpenAM's first-run configuration wizard in the browser before the module will work** — the JATO ViewBean endpoints, including the vulnerable password-reset form, are not reachable until initial setup finishes.

When testing against a local container like this instead of a remote HTTPS deployment, override the module's HTTPS-oriented defaults accordingly:

```
set RHOSTS 127.0.0.1
set RPORT 8080
set SSL false
set LHOST <attacker-lan-ip>
set LPORT 4444
set ReverseListenerBindAddress 0.0.0.0
set SRVPORT 8081
```

- `RPORT 8080` / `SSL false` match the vendor image's default plain-HTTP listener (the module otherwise defaults to `443`/`SSL true` for a typical production deployment).
- `ReverseListenerBindAddress 0.0.0.0` with an explicit `LHOST` is useful when the Metasploit host itself has multiple interfaces (e.g. a Docker bridge network plus your LAN) — it binds the reverse listener on every interface while still advertising the one address the target container can actually reach back.
- `SRVPORT` only matters for the Linux Meterpreter dropper target (`TARGET 1`); point it at a free local port if `CmdStager`'s default collides with something already listening — here, OpenAM itself already owns 8080.

## Verification Steps

1. Stand up a vulnerable OpenAM instance as described in **Setup** above (or point at an existing OpenAM <= 16.0.5 instance).
2. Ensure the vulnerable JATO ViewBean endpoint is reachable.
3. Start `msfconsole`.
4. Run `use exploit/multi/http/openam_cve_2026_33439`.
5. Set `RHOSTS` to the target host.
6. Set `RPORT` and `SSL` as required.
7. Set `TARGETURI` if OpenAM is not deployed under `/openam`.
8. Run `check`.
9. Select target `0` for direct Unix command execution or target `1` for a Linux x64 Meterpreter payload.
10. Set the payload connection options and run the module.

When using the Linux dropper against an environment where the staging service presents an untrusted or self-signed TLS certificate, set:

```text
set CMDSTAGER::FLAVOR printf
```

This avoids relying on the target's `curl` or `wget` client to trust the staging service certificate.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->5d:Td6e,## Description

OptInt accepts any integer, so a module that wanted a bounded value (a loop count, a thread count, etc.) had to range-check it itself after the datastore had already accepted it. This adds optional minimum: and maximum: keywords that reject out-of-range values as part of normal option validation:

    OptInt.new('COUNT', [true, 'Loop count', 3], minimum: 1, maximum: 50)

Either bound can be given on its own. The range is appended to the option description so it shows in `info`, following the existing max_length convention. Passing minimum greater than maximum raises at definition time instead of silently rejecting everything. Options without a range behave exactly as before.

**Related Issue:** Closes #21732

Kept it to OptInt as the issue asked - OptFloat could get the same later. OptPort already has its own 0..65535 check and subclasses OptInt, so it picks up minimum/maximum for free too - that's a feature, not a regression, since the bounds default to nil.

## Breaking Changes

None. minimum/maximum default to nil, so existing OptInt.new(...) calls are unaffected.

## Reviewer Notes

The whole change is in lib/msf/core/opt_int.rb: an initialize that pulls minimum:/maximum: out and forwards the rest to super, a range check inside the existing valid?, and a small helper for the description suffix. Named minimum:/maximum: to match the existing convention in OptIntRange (`maximum:`, opt_int_range.rb). One thing worth noting: a failed range check surfaces as the shared "One or more options failed to validate" message, since options only return a boolean from valid? - there's no per-option "out of range" string, which is consistent with every other option type. A default that falls outside the bounds is not rejected at definition time; it's rejected the first time the option is validated. Behaviour against a ranged option is otherwise identical to an unbounded OptInt.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/opt_int_spec.rb - all examples pass, including the new range cases.
2. - [x] A ranged OptInt rejects out-of-range values on validation and shows the range in the description. Verified against the real framework code path (Msf::OptionContainer#validate, the same call msfconsole makes on run): `set COUNT 0` raises "One or more options failed to validate: COUNT."; `set COUNT 50` is accepted and normalized to Integer; `desc` -> "Loop count (range: 1-50)". msfconsole also boots cleanly with the change.
3. - [x] Existing OptInt options without a range still accept any integer, including hex and negatives - covered by the shared "an option" examples and the unbounded case in the spec.

## Test Evidence

Added coverage in spec/lib/msf/core/opt_int_spec.rb for both bounds, below-min/above-max rejection, negatives, hex within range, minimum-only and maximum-only, a single allowed value (min == max), zero-value bounds, an out-of-range default, the description suffix, the min>max guard, and a no-range backward-compat case. The existing shared "an option" examples still apply.

Verified locally on Ruby 3.3.12 (homebrew, arm64-darwin): `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/opt_int_spec.rb spec/lib/msf/core/opt_port_spec.rb` -> 53 examples, 0 failures. 5e:Tec7,## Description

`Net::NTLM::Message::Type3#domain` and `#user` hand back the raw security buffer bytes, no decoding at all (checked directly in `rubyntlm`, the accessor is a plain `self['name'].value`). When the client negotiated `NTLMSSP_NEGOTIATE_UNICODE`, those bytes are UTF-16LE, i.e. every character has a null byte after it.

`handle_type3` in the HTTP NTLM relay server client builds the relay identity like this:

```ruby
domain = parsed_type3.domain.to_s.force_encoding('UTF-8')
user = parsed_type3.user.to_s.force_encoding('UTF-8')
identity = "#{domain}\\#{user}" unless user.empty?
```

`force_encoding` only changes the label Ruby puts on the bytes, it doesn't transcode anything. So the null bytes from the UTF-16LE wire format stay in the string.

Why it matters: `modules/auxiliary/server/relay/esc8.rb`, in `MODE AUTO`, decides whether it's looking at a machine account with:

```ruby
cert_template = relay_identity.end_with?('$') ? ['DomainController', 'Machine'] : ['User']
```

With this bug a real machine account like `DOMAIN\DC1$` never satisfies `end_with?('$')`, because the actual last byte in the identity string is a trailing null, not the dollar sign. A coerced machine-account relay silently gets treated as a regular user account and requests the wrong certificate template. Confirmed against a real DC in a lab (`KERBEROS\DC1$`, coerced via PetitPotam, relayed through `smb_to_ldap.rb`): the identity logged with a literal null byte after every character.

```ruby
"D\x00C\x001\x00$\x00".dup.force_encoding("UTF-8").end_with?("$")
# => false
```

The fix transcodes based on the flag that was actually negotiated instead of relabeling:

```ruby
def decode_ntlm_field(raw, message)
  return '' if raw.nil?

  bytes = raw.to_s.dup
  if message.has_flag?(:UNICODE)
    bytes.force_encoding('UTF-16LE').encode('UTF-8')
  else
    bytes.force_encoding('Windows-1252').encode('UTF-8')
  end
rescue EncodingError
  raw.to_s.dup.force_encoding('UTF-8')
end
```

Used for both `domain` and `user` in `handle_type3`. Windows-1252 for the non-UNICODE (OEM) branch is a practical default, not a protocol guarantee, the OEM codepage is negotiated out of band. It is correct for the ASCII identities this fix targets, not necessarily universal for non-ASCII OEM strings.

**Related Issue:** Fixes #21919

## Breaking Changes

None.

## Reviewer Notes

The same pattern (`.to_s.force_encoding('UTF-8')`) is also being added to `lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay/ntlm/server_client.rb` by the still-open #21908, which copies this file's approach. That PR isn't merged, so master isn't broken by it today, but it will need the same treatment, either as a followup here or folded into #21908 before it merges. Happy to widen this into a shared helper if that's preferred.

## Verification Steps

1. - [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/http_server/relay/ntlm/server_client_spec.rb` passes.
2. - [x] A UTF-16LE machine-account name (`DC1$`) decodes to clean UTF-8 with no embedded nulls and `end_with?('$')` is true.
3. - [x] An OEM (non-unicode) name decodes as a single-byte encoding rather than being misread as UTF-16LE.
4. - [x] `nil` and malformed/odd-length buffers fall back instead of raising out of the relay.

## Test Evidence

New spec covers: UTF-16LE decode with no embedded nulls and `end_with?('$')` true; OEM single-byte decode; `nil` returns empty; malformed buffer falls back; and a `DOMAIN\user` identity round-trip.

```
$ bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/http_server/relay/ntlm/server_client_spec.rb
6 examples, 0 failures
```

Passes locally and in CI. (An earlier note here mentioned a local `nio4r` build failure blocking the suite; that was an environment-only toolchain issue and no longer applies.)
5f:T14ff,## Verification

When exactly one relay target is configured, `ServerClient#do_session_setup_smb2` enters relay mode on the very first `SessionSetup` message ("Prepare the relay now, if there's only one target to relay to and this is the first session setup message"). That skips the normal non-relay flow, which is the *only* other place `session.metadata[:identity]` gets populated (`session.metadata[:identity] = session.user_id`, right after `super`).

Consequence: a genuinely successful single-target relay never has an identity, so it's misreported as anonymous:

```ruby
if session.metadata[:identity].blank?
  logger.print_status("Anonymous Identity - Successfully authenticated against relay target #{relayed_connection.target}")
  relayed_connection.disconnect!
else
  logger.print_good("Identity: #{session.metadata[:identity]} - Successfully authenticated against relay target #{relayed_connection.target}")
  @listener.on_relay_success(relay_connection: relayed_connection, relay_identity: session.metadata[:identity])
end
```

`@listener.on_relay_success` never fires for these relays, even for a real, named, successfully-authenticated user.

There's a second-order effect too: because the relay outcome is recorded against a `nil` identity (`@relay_targets.on_relay_end(..., identity: session.metadata[:identity], ...)`), and `TargetList::Target#eligible_relay_target?` treats `nil` identity as always eligible (`return true if identity.nil?`), the target stays "eligible" after a successful relay. The following `TREE_CONNECT` re-selects the same target and returns `FORCE_RETRY_SESSION_SETUP` (`STATUS_NETWORK_SESSION_EXPIRED`) - which is exactly the status code this single-target fast path was introduced to avoid sending, per #20967: "Not all smbclients respond with a reauthentication attempt to the error code `STATUS_NETWORK_SESSION_EXPIRED`."

This matches the original PR's own test output, from before the later anonymous-success guard was added in #21390:

```
I, [...] INFO -- : Relaying NTLMv2 type 3 message to smb://172.16.199.200:445 as
[+] 172.16.199.200:445 - Identity:  - Successfully authenticated against relay target smb://172.16.199.200:445
[SMB] NTLMv2-SSP Username   : KERBEROS\Administrator
```

The identity was empty even then; it just wasn't consequential until #21390 added the blank-identity guard around `on_relay_success`.

## Fix

Derive `session.metadata[:identity]` from the incoming Type3 message's `domain`/`user` before it's used, only when not already set (so the existing multi-target flow, which already populates identity via the non-relay path, is untouched). This mirrors the pattern the HTTP NTLM relay target already uses successfully in `handle_type3`.

## Proof / PoC

Added `spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay/ntlm/server_client_spec.rb`, driving `do_session_setup_smb2` through a real (not stubbed) NTLM Type1 -> Type3 exchange with exactly one configured relay target:

- Successful relay with a named identity (`CORP\victim`) now populates `session.metadata[:identity]` and fires `on_relay_success` with that identity.
- The relay outcome is recorded in `TargetList` against the real identity, not `nil`, and the target is correctly excluded from re-selection for that identity afterward (`TargetList#next` returns `nil`, so `TREE_CONNECT` no longer forces a redundant retry).
- A genuinely anonymous relay (blank domain/user) is still correctly reported as anonymous - the fix doesn't change that case.

To confirm the tests actually catch the regression (and not just pass trivially), I ran them against the pre-fix code too: 3 of 4 fail without this change, and the anonymous-case test still correctly passes either way.

```
$ bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay/ntlm/server_client_spec.rb
4 examples, 0 failures

$ bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/relay spec/lib/msf/exploit/remote/smb/relay
62 examples, 0 failures
```

No behavior change for the multi-target case (verified: with 2+ targets configured, the single-target fast-path branch that skips normal identity population is never entered, so it never reaches this code path in a different state than before).

## Testing

- [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay/ntlm/server_client_spec.rb` - 4 examples, 0 failures
- [x] `bundle exec rspec spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/relay spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/relay spec/lib/msf/exploit/remote/smb/relay` - 62 examples, 0 failures (no regressions)
- [x] Rubocop on changed files - no new offenses introduced
- [x] Verified the new tests fail on the pre-fix code (regression-proof) and pass after the fix

This module exploits [CVE-2025-57231](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-59m3-fj8c-996g), a directory traversal vulnerability in Docmost that allows arbitrary file read from version 0.2.1 to 0.21.0. The application's `/api/attachments/img/avatar` endpoint does not properly validate file paths and is publicly accessible without any authentication. This allows unauthenticated attackers to retrieve arbitrary file contents from the target by probing the `/avatar` endpoint with %2F sequences.

## Breaking Changes

None

## Reviewer Notes

I could not find a way to include a check method for this module.

## Verification Steps

1. Set up Docmost version 0.21.0 (instructions are given in the module documentation)
2. Start `msfconsole` and type use `auxiliary/gather/docmost_fileread_cve_2025_57231`
3. Set target IP, target port and target URI with `set RHOSTS`, `set RPORT` and `set TARGETURI`
4. Execute `run`. It should check if the target is online and locally save the contents of arbitrary file path.

## Test Evidence

The module was tested with Docmost versions 0.21.0 and 0.22.0(patched). For version 0.21.0, the module correctly dumps the contents of arbitrary file location. For version 0.22.0, the module correctly fails by displaying 400 status code.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->62:T86f,## Summary

Adds an unauthenticated RCE module for CVE-2026-76071 affecting Netis NC63
firmware through V3.0.0.3327.

The `ipFilterList=mod` handler parses the second `destHost` field with a
widthless `sscanf` scanset into a fixed-size stack buffer. The module overwrites
the saved MIPS return address with the firmware `system()` PLT entry and uses an
attacker-controlled final parameter name as the command argument.

The firmware provides `wget`, so the module uses a MIPS little-endian Linux
dropper target and was demonstrated with
`linux/mipsle/meterpreter/reverse_tcp`.

## Verification

Validated against the original-hash V3.0.0.3327 `netis.cgi` in an isolated
QEMU user-mode environment:

- Original CGI command execution returned `uid=0 gid=0 groups=0`.
- The `wget; chmod; payload` command stager downloaded and executed the MIPSLE
  Meterpreter payload.
- A Meterpreter session opened and filesystem access plus process execution
  were verified.
- `ruby tools/dev/msftidy.rb modules/exploits/linux/http/netis_nc63_ipfilterlist_rce_cve_2026_76071.rb`
- `ruby tools/dev/msftidy_docs.rb documentation/modules/exploit/linux/http/netis_nc63_ipfilterlist_rce_cve_2026_76071.md`

The test `netis.cgi` SHA-256 was:
`eb298774c27070dc595fefcabb4e8c12a46cb5f4fd08f91c3ca92282c3a289a2`.

The QEMU user-mode setup used static BusyBox compatibility executables only for
recursive child execution (`/bin/sh`, `wget`, `chmod`, and `rm`). The original
firmware binary, vulnerable parsing, ROP control flow, `system()` command,
MIPSLE payload download, and reverse session were retained. The module is
therefore ranked Manual and its documentation includes the limitation. No
Internet-reachable device was tested.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->63:T219c,Refering to https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-framework/issues/20506

## Description

This PR introduces **`test_env`**, a new Metasploit plugin that automates the provisioning, management, and validation of vulnerable test environments for exploit and auxiliary modules.

### Development History

This PR bundles work that was reviewed incrementally over 12 weekly deliverable PRs on [my fork](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework). If you'd like to see the progression of the work instead of one large diff, each week is linked below:

- [Week 1](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/1) 
- [Week 2](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/3)
- [Week 3](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/4) 
- [Week 4](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/5) 
- [Week 5](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/6) 
- [Week 6](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/7) 
- [Week 7](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/8) 
- [Week 8](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/9) 
- [Week 9](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/10) 
- [Week 10](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/11) 
- [Week 11](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/12) 
- [Week 12](https://github.com/Nayeraneru/metasploit-framework/pull/15) 

### What it does
- **`test_env build`** — Reads a module's `VulnerableEnvironment` metadata, resolves a YAML environment definition, pulls the container image, dynamically allocates free host ports, launches the container bound to `127.0.0.1`, waits for health checks, runs one-time provisioning for images that boot unconfigured (e.g., automatically submitting the WordPress install wizard), and auto-configures the module's datastore (`RHOSTS`, `RPORT`, credentials, etc.).
- **`test_env exec <ID>`** — Loads the module an environment was built for, applies the stored datastore, automatically sets the recommended payload from the definition's `ci.exploit` metadata when the module's default is incompatible with the image, and runs the exploit.
- **`test_env validate <ID>`** — Verifies the exploit produced the expected result (session type, command output) against the definition's `ci.validation` metadata.
- **`test_env modules`** — Scans the framework and lists all modules that declare `VulnerableEnvironment` support.
- **`test_env list / stop / start / remove / remove-all`** — Lifecycle management of tracked environments.
- **`test_env status`** — Runtime diagnostics and definition validation.

### Architecture
- **Runtime abstraction layer** (`BaseRuntime` → `DockerRuntime` / `PodmanRuntime`) with auto-detection and rootless Podman validation.
- **YAML environment definitions** (`data/vuln_envs/*.yml`) that declare images, ports, credentials, health checks, provisioning steps, and CI metadata.
- **ActiveModel-backed YAML registry** (`~/.msf4/test_env_registry.yml`) with file locking for safe multi-process access, plus container label-based state reconstruction across `msfconsole` restarts.
- **`VulnerableEnvironment` metadata** — Modules declare their test environment via a new key in `update_info()`, following the same pattern as `Name`, `Description`, `Notes`, etc.
- **Comprehensive documentation** — Includes both User Documentation (`USER_Doc.md`) for interactive usage workflows and Developer Documentation (`DEVELOPER_Doc.md`) for authoring new environment definitions and module integrations.

### CI Integration
Integrates `test_env` into GitHub Actions, replacing static container definitions with environment-driven provisioning. The CI matrix consumes the same YAML definitions as local users, validating that shared definitions, health checks, datastore automation, and exploit execution work identically across local dev and CI.

### Files changed
- **Architecture design** (documented): `01-command-dispatcher`, `02-module-metadata`, `03-database-schema`, `04-environment-schema`, `05-runtime-adapter`
- **`plugins/test_env.rb`** — The plugin (runtime adapters, port allocator, health manager, provisioner, registry, command dispatcher).
- **`data/vuln_envs/activemq.yml`** — Apache ActiveMQ Classic (CVE-2026-34197) with Jolokia API.
- **`data/vuln_envs/wordpress.yml`** — Vulnerable WordPress with one-time install wizard provisioning.
- **`data/vuln_envs/httpd.yml`** — Apache HTTP Server for scanner testing.
- **`data/vuln_envs/openssh.yml`** — OpenSSH server for SSH scanner testing.
- **`modules/exploits/multi/http/apache_activemq_jolokia_rce.rb`, `modules/exploits/multi/misc/apache_activemq_rce_cve_2023_46604.rb`, `modules/exploits/unix/webapp/wp_admin_shell_upload.rb`, `auxiliary/scanner/http/http_version`, `auxiliary/scanner/http/http_header`, `auxiliary/scanner/http/robots_txt`, `auxiliary/scanner/ssh/ssh_version`,** — Added `VulnerableEnvironment` metadata as the reference module integration.
- **`.github/workflows/test_env.yml`** — GitHub Actions CI matrix validating all environment definitions.
- **`scripts/ci_runner.sh`** — Headless msfconsole runner for CI with Unix exit-code bridging.
- **`DEVELOPER_Doc.md`** — Complete schema reference, three-level merge hierarchy, and authoring guide for environment definitions.
- **`USER_Doc.md`** — Interactive usage guide, command reference, troubleshooting, and workflow examples for end users.

### Why
Manually building and configuring Docker containers for every exploit test is repetitive, error-prone, and a barrier to reproducible security research. This plugin makes it one command, enables multi-version testing, aligns module definitions with CI validation expectations, and ensures local development and CI share the same environment definitions.

## Breaking Changes

None. This is a self-contained plugin. No existing APIs, mixins, or datastore options are modified. Modules opt-in by adding `VulnerableEnvironment` to their `update_info()` metadata

## Verification Steps

1. - [ ] Ensure Docker or Podman is installed and running.
2. - [ ] Start `msfconsole` and load the plugin:
     ```
     msf > load test_env
     ```
     **Expected:** `TestEnv plugin loaded. Runtime: docker` (or `podman`).
3. - [ ] Check runtime status and definition validation:
     ```
     msf > test_env status
     ```
     **Expected:** Lists available definitions (`activemq`, `httpd`, `openssh`, `wordpress`) with no validation errors.
4. - [ ] Discover modules with test_env support:
     ```
     msf > test_env modules
     ```
     **Expected:** Lists supported modules including `exploit/multi/http/apache_activemq_jolokia_rce`, `exploit/unix/webapp/wp_admin_shell_upload`, and auxiliary scanners.
5. - [ ] Select the reference module and build its environment:
     ```
     msf > use exploit/multi/http/apache_activemq_jolokia_rce
     msf exploit(...) > test_env build
     ```
     **Expected:** Image is pulled, container starts, health check passes, and the command prints:
     ```
     [+] Environment ready.
     [*] Environment ID: 1
        RHOSTS       => 127.0.0.1
        RPORT        => <dynamic_port>
        TARGETURI    => /
        USERNAME     => admin
        PASSWORD     => admin
     ```
6. - [ ] List tracked environments:
     ```
     msf > test_env list
     ```
     **Expected:** Table shows environment `1` with status `running`.
7. - [ ] Execute the exploit against the built environment:
     ```
     msf > test_env exec 1
     ```
     **Expected:** Module loads automatically, the recommended payload (`cmd/linux/http/x64/meterpreter/reverse_tcp`) is applied from the environment definition because the module's default is incompatible with this image, and the exploit runs with the stored datastore. A Meterpreter session opens (if `LHOST` is reachable).
8. - [ ] Validate the exploit result:
     ```
     msf > test_env validate 1
     ```
     **Expected:** `PASS: environment 1 validated successfully against activemq's ci.validation.`
9. - [ ] Stop, start, and remove the environment:
     ```
     msf > test_env stop 1
     msf > test_env start 1
     msf > test_env remove 1
     ```
     **Expected:** Each command reports success. `test_env list` shows no environments after removal.
10. - [ ] Test `remove-all` cleanup:
     ```
     msf > test_env build
     msf > test_env remove-all
     ```
     **Expected:** All containers stopped/removed, registry reset.

This module exploits [CVE-2022-4140](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2022-4140), an arbitrary file read vulnerability in WordPress's Welcart e-Commerce plugin(versions < 2.8.5). The plugin's content-log.php file does not validate user input before using it to output the content of logfiles. <!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->67:T9db,## Summary
Fixes three medium-severity findings from an internal security scan of this repo:

- **SNMP MIB import (CWE-78, CWE-95)**: `SNMP::MIB.import_module` interpolated a caller-controlled filename into a shell command and `eval`'d the smidump converter output. Now runs `smidump` via `Open3.popen3` with an argv array and parses the output with a new `SNMP::PythonLiteralParser` instead of `eval`.
- **ZIP extraction path traversal (CWE-22)**: `is_child_of?` used a raw `start_with?` string check, so a sibling directory like `<tmp>_evil/` passed containment. Now compares expanded paths for exact equality or requires the extraction root followed by a path separator.
- **RPC pre-auth resource exhaustion (CWE-400)**: `msfrpcd` decoded the full unauthenticated request body with `MessagePack.unpack` before any size limit or auth check. Added a 10MB ceiling enforced in the HTTP packet parser (before the body is buffered) and re-checked in `Msf::RPC::Service#process` before unpacking.

Each fix has an accompanying test (`spec/lib/snmp/mib_spec.rb`, `spec/support/shared/examples/msf/db_manager/import/metasploit_framework/zip.rb`, `spec/lib/rex/proto/http/packet_spec.rb`, `spec/lib/msf/core/rpc/v10/service_spec.rb`).

## Test plan
- [x] Verified `SNMP::PythonLiteralParser` directly against real MIB-shaped input and injected backtick/method-call payloads that the previous `eval` would have executed
- [x] Verified `is_child_of?` directly: accepts root/descendants, rejects sibling-prefix paths
- [x] Verified by code inspection that the RPC/HTTP size guard runs before `MessagePack.unpack`
- [ ] Full `bundle exec rspec` run (blocked in dev environment: no network, vendored gems incomplete — CI should run the added specs)

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->68:T1209,## Description

This PR pulls in a custom rex-text branch, which adds a Hierarchy Table class.

This PR spikes out a new way (actually, two ways) to show the registered service hierarchy as part of the `services` command.
This PR also addressed some shortcomings of the udp_sweep module, whereby it did not register the service hierarchies at all. I used this for testing locally, but can extract this piece of work to a different PR.

- Keep ASCII and Unicode, or remove one of them
- Keep tree and group mode, or remove one of them

```6c:T128f,## Summary

Adds `auxiliary/scanner/scada/opcua_endpoint_enum`, which enumerates the endpoints an OPC-UA server advertises and reports the security posture of each one.

This follows up #21612 (`opcua_enum`), which detects OPC-UA servers via the HEL/ACK handshake. This module goes a step further: it opens a secure channel with `SecurityPolicy=None` and calls `GetEndpoints`, then reports each endpoint's URL, `MessageSecurityMode`, `SecurityPolicyUri`, and accepted `UserIdentityToken` types, plus the server's `ApplicationUri` and `ProductUri` as a fingerprint.

Endpoints accepting the Anonymous identity token over a channel with `MessageSecurityMode` None are flagged and recorded via `report_vuln` - any host that can reach the port can connect with no credentials over an unencrypted channel, which is generally enough to read live process data and, depending on node permissions, write it.

## Why a separate module rather than an action on opcua_enum

`opcua_enum` succeeds against servers where this one fails, so it isn't superseded. HEL/ACK is a transport handshake with no security negotiation, while `GetEndpoints` requires an `OpenSecureChannel`. The specification requires the discovery endpoint to accept `SecurityPolicy=None` precisely so clients can learn how to connect, but hardened deployments restrict it. `opcua_enum` remains the detector that works when the channel is closed.

## Verification

Tested against two independent OPC-UA implementations:

**node-opcua** (7 endpoints, 3 security policy families, includes an unsecured endpoint):

```
msf6 auxiliary(scanner/scada/opcua_endpoint_enum) > run

[+] 127.0.0.1:4842        - OPC-UA server enumerated - 7 endpoint(s), 1 unauthenticated and unencrypted
[*] 127.0.0.1:4842        -   [0] opc.tcp://190d0c6ea827:4840/UA/BackdraftTest
[*] 127.0.0.1:4842        -       security: None/None  identity: UserName, Certificate, Anonymous
[!] 127.0.0.1:4842        -       endpoint accepts anonymous clients over an unencrypted channel
[*] 127.0.0.1:4842        -   [1] opc.tcp://190d0c6ea827:4840/UA/BackdraftTest
[*] 127.0.0.1:4842        -       security: Basic256Sha256/Sign  identity: UserName, Certificate, Anonymous
...
[*] 127.0.0.1:4842        -   ApplicationUri: urn:190d0c6ea827:NodeOPCUA-Server
```

The parsed endpoint list matches the server's own startup log line for line, including ordering and the Aes128/Aes256 policy URIs.

**Inductive Automation Ignition 8.3.4** (3 endpoints, all Basic256Sha256/SignAndEncrypt, no unsecured endpoint):

```
[+] 10.10.0.3:62541       - OPC-UA server enumerated - 3 endpoint(s), 0 unauthenticated and unencrypted
[*] 10.10.0.3:62541       -   [0] opc.tcp://bd-83-primary:62541
[*] 10.10.0.3:62541       -       security: Basic256Sha256/SignAndEncrypt  identity: UserName
```

Worth noting that the endpoint list is returned even though no endpoint offers the None policy - the discovery channel is open by specification regardless of what the server's real endpoints require.

Output was also diffed field by field against an independent Python implementation using the `asyncua` stack, and matches.

## Notes for reviewers

- No new gem dependencies. Binary framing is hand-built with `pack`/`unpack`, following the `iec104` style.
- Reads accumulate until the full message length is received. `GetEndpoints` responses carry a certificate per endpoint and routinely span TCP segments; the largest observed was 10858 bytes.
- Chunk reassembly is implemented but has not been exercised in testing - every server tested negotiated a buffer large enough to return a single `F` chunk. That path is written from Part 6 rather than validated on the wire.
- Array lengths and all reads are bounds-checked, so a malformed or hostile response raises a parse error rather than allocating without bound.
- `CloseSecureChannel` is sent so channels are released rather than left to expire.
- Docker recipes for both test servers are in the module documentation.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->6e:Tba0,Fixes #21721

## Problem

On Ruby 3.4+ (e.g. the Omnibus installer), every PacketFu-based module crashes:

```
[-] Auxiliary failed: NoMethodError undefined method '[]' for nil
[-]   packetfu-2.0.0/lib/packetfu/structfu.rb:20:in 'StructFu#typecast'
[-]   packetfu-2.0.0/lib/packetfu/protos/eth/header.rb:168:in 'PacketFu::EthHeader#eth_proto='
```

## Root cause

`StructFu#typecast` determines the attribute being assigned by regex-parsing the
immediate caller backtrace frame for the pre-Ruby-3.4 format (`file.rb:168:in `attr='`).
Ruby 3.4 changed backtrace formatting to single quotes with qualified method names
(`file.rb:168:in 'Class#attr='`), so the match returns nil and `nil[1]` raises. This
affects every protocol header setter in packetfu 2.0.0 (its latest release, 2023).

Framework CI's default job runs `.ruby-version` (3.3.8), and no unit test exercises
packet header *setters*, so the bug slipped past the existing Ruby 3.4 CI job.

## Why the IPv6-only fix is not enough

Commit 1302e6512 (from #21725) added a `StructFu#typecast` override in
`lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/ipv6.rb`. That file is only autoloaded when the IPv6 mixin
is referenced, and `defer_module_loads` defaults to `true` in 6.4+
(`lib/msf/core/feature_manager.rb`). Capture-based modules
(`auxiliary/scanner/discovery/arp_sweep`, `auxiliary/spoof/cisco/dtp`,
`auxiliary/server/icmp_exfil`, ...) include `Msf::Exploit::Remote::Capture` and never
touch the IPv6 mixin, so they still crash on Ruby 3.4. This PR applies the shim wherever
`packetfu` is loaded and replaces the IPv6-only inline override with the shared
implementation.

## Fix

`Msf::PacketFuCompat.apply!` prepends a Ruby 3.4-aware `typecast` onto `StructFu`. It is
applied from both PacketFu entry points (`capture.rb` and `ipv6.rb`) and is idempotent.
It can be removed once the framework depends on a PacketFu release containing the
upstream fix (packetfu/packetfu#218).

## Verification

Verified against real Ruby 3.4.11 with packetfu 2.0.0:

```
UNPATCHED: ARPPacket=NoMethodError UDPPacket=NoMethodError TCPPacket=NoMethodError ICMPPacket=NoMethodError IPv6Packet=NoMethodError
applied=idempotent
PATCHED  : ARPPacket=ok UDPPacket=ok TCPPacket=ok ICMPPacket=ok IPv6Packet=ok
regex match => ["eth_proto"]
```

New specs cover pre-3.4 frames, Ruby 3.4 qualified and singleton frames, unrelated-frame
skipping, idempotency, the informative failure mode, and construction of the real packet
headers that raise without the shim.

The `build / mettle ubuntu-latest` acceptance job fails intermittently on master
(`[-] Post interrupted by the console user`) and is unrelated to this change.

<img width="1366" height="736" alt="Screenshot from 2026-08-02 19-06-15" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/bf07d2ec-23c5-4f53-b1e7-0dc7e5bc7919" />76:Tece,## Summary

Depends on #21717 and #21729.

This PR adds application-protocol carrier tracing for Kerberos service authentication over HTTP, SMB, LDAP, and MSSQL.

It builds on the service-authentication trace events and presentation introduced by #21729. The carrier callbacks describe where GSS-Kerberos or SPNEGO tokens are transported by each application protocol, while reusing the shared token parsing from #21717.

This is a stacked PR. Until its dependencies are merged, the GitHub diff against `master` also includes the commits from #21717 and #21729.

## Functionality

This adds 10 request/response carrier callbacks:

| Protocol | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| HTTP | `Authorization` header | `WWW-Authenticate` header |
| SMB1 | Session Setup request security blob | Session Setup response security blob |
| SMB2 | Session Setup request security blob | Session Setup response buffer |
| LDAP | SASL initial credential | `serverSaslCreds` |
| MSSQL | TDS7 Login SSPI security blob | TDS login response metadata |

### HTTP

- Traces outgoing `Authorization` headers.
- Traces incoming `WWW-Authenticate` headers.
- Records the authentication scheme and Base64/decoded token lengths.
- Handles both `Kerberos` and `Negotiate` response schemes.

### SMB

- Traces SMB1 Session Setup request and response security blobs.
- Traces SMB2 Session Setup request security blobs and response buffers.
- Records the SMB version, direction, field name, and blob metadata.

### LDAP

- Traces the SASL bind `initial_credential`.
- Traces the returned `serverSaslCreds`.
- Works with the existing GSS-SPNEGO LDAP authentication flow, including channel binding and sign/seal setup.

### MSSQL

- Traces the SSPI security blob placed in the TDS7 Login packet.
- Records metadata for the corresponding TDS login response.

## Carrier presentation

The Kerberos subscriber interface now includes:

- `on_protocol_carrier`

`ServiceAuthenticationTracePresenter` presents carrier metadata including, when available:

- Protocol and direction
- Carrier and field/header name
- HTTP authentication scheme
- Host and port
- Service principal
- Encoded and decoded token lengths
- Security-blob length
- GSS-Kerberos or SPNEGO token summary

Carrier tokens are identified as:

- GSS-Kerberos
- SPNEGO `NegTokenInit`
- SPNEGO `NegTokenResp`

Malformed or unsupported carrier tokens are shown as structured parse failures.

## Trace modes

Protocol carriers are shown in the existing `metadata` and `full` trace modes:

- `metadata` reports structured carrier and token metadata while redacting binary contents.
- `full` includes hexadecimal binary token contents.

Tracing failures are isolated from the authentication flow and do not interrupt authentication.

## Scope

This PR owns the generic protocol-carrier event, its presentation, and the HTTP, SMB, LDAP, and MSSQL integrations.

It does not introduce:

- Low-level GSS/SPNEGO token handling, which belongs to #21717.
- AP-REQ, AP-REP, KRB-ERROR, mutual-authentication, or service-ticket trace presentation, which belongs to #21729.

## Testing

Direct unit tests cover all four protocol integrations and their request/response callbacks, together with the carrier subscriber and presenter behavior.

The following affected test suites were run:

```bash
bundle exec rspec \
  spec/lib/rex/proto/kerberos \
  spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/kerberos \
  spec/lib/msf/base/serializer/readable_text_spec.rb \
  spec/lib/rex/proto/http/client_spec.rb \
  spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/smb/client/kerberos_authentication_spec.rb \
  spec/lib/rex/proto/ldap/auth_adapter/rex_kerberos_spec.rb \
  spec/lib/rex/proto/mssql/client_spec.rb77:T12d6,## Summary

Depends on #21717.

This PR extends `KerberosTicketTrace` to cover the Kerberos service-authentication exchange after a service ticket has been selected.

It builds on `Rex::Proto::Gss::KerberosToken`, introduced by #21717, and adds structured tracing for AP-REQ construction, GSS-Kerberos and SPNEGO encapsulation, and service-authentication responses.

Protocol-specific HTTP, SMB, LDAP, and MSSQL carrier hooks are intentionally excluded and will be submitted separately.

## Functionality

The service authenticator now emits structured trace events for:

- AP-REQ construction and the selected service ticket
- GSS-Kerberos AP-REQ encapsulation
- SPNEGO `NegTokenInit` encapsulation
- GSS-Kerberos AP-REP responses
- Kerberos error responses
- SPNEGO `NegTokenResp` responses
- Mutual-authentication trace results, when mutual authentication is requested:
  - `success` after an AP-REP is parsed and decrypted
  - `failed` when the Kerberos response fails parsing/decryption or negotiation is rejected
  - `incomplete` when SPNEGO negotiation succeeds but no AP-REP has been validated in that trace event

The trace context includes, when available:

- Client and service principals
- SPN and realm
- Selected service-ticket metadata
- Ticket encryption type and flags
- Ticket validity times
- AP options
- Authenticator checksum
- Authenticator subkey and sequence number
- Replay-detection state
- Channel-binding presence and length
- Delegated-credential state

Malformed or unsupported tokens are rendered as structured parse failures without changing the existing authentication error behavior.

## Trace presentation

The presentation and parsing responsibilities are separated into dedicated components:

- `Rex::Proto::Kerberos::GssTokenParser`
  - Adapts the shared token handling introduced by #21717 for trace presentation.
  - Parses GSS-Kerberos, SPNEGO `NegTokenInit`, and SPNEGO `NegTokenResp` metadata.

- `Rex::Proto::Kerberos::ServiceAuthenticationTracePresenter`
  - Presents AP-REQ, GSS/SPNEGO, AP-REP, and KRB-ERROR trace events.
  - Handles mutual-authentication and service-ticket context.

- `Rex::Proto::Kerberos::KerberosTraceSerializer`
  - Serializes Kerberos model objects, flags, enums, timestamps, and binary values into trace-friendly output.

`KerberosLoggerSubscriber` is reduced to trace-mode handling, event routing, headers, colors, and output dispatch.

## Trace modes

The existing `KerberosTicketTrace` modes are preserved:

| Mode | Service-authentication output |
|---|---|
| `off` | No trace output |
| `metadata` | Structured metadata with binary contents redacted |
| `ticket` | The selected service-ticket credential |
| `full` | Full AP-REQ/authenticator details and hexadecimal binary contents |

Binary token payloads are summarized in metadata mode. Kerberos model fields, including checksums, keys, and encrypted fields, are serialized normally; printable string values may be shown, and non-printable binary strings are summarized.

## Subscriber events

The Kerberos subscriber interface now supports:

- `on_ap_req`
- `on_gss_token`
- `on_spnego_token`
- `on_response_token`

These events keep service-authentication trace presentation separate from the authentication implementation.

## Additional changes

- Corrects the AP-REP success message that previously referred to an AP-REQ.
- Updates the `KerberosTicketTrace` option description from ticket tracing to protocol tracing.
- Ensures temporary AP-REQ/authenticator trace context is removed before returning the authentication result.

## Scope

This PR is limited to Kerberos service-authentication trace collection and presentation.

It does not add application-protocol carrier callbacks. HTTP, SMB, LDAP, and MSSQL integration will follow in a separate dependent PR.

## Testing

The following affected test suites were run:

```bash
bundle exec rspec \
  spec/lib/rex/proto/kerberos \
  spec/lib/msf/core/exploit/remote/kerberos \
  spec/lib/msf/base/serializer/readable_text_spec.rb

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->78:T5d7,### Summary

Adds an exploit module for CVE-2025-54253 (CVSS 10.0, CISA KEV, exploited in the wild): Adobe Experience Manager (AEM) Forms on JEE 6.5.23.0 and earlier ship the Apache Struts development-mode debug servlet exposed and unauthenticated at `/adminui/debug` (and `/lc/adminui/debug`). The servlet evaluates the OGNL expression in the `expression` request parameter and reflects the result, enabling unauthenticated OGNL evaluation and OS command execution.

The module:
- confirms OGNL evaluation with a benign string-concatenation probe (no process spawned);
- executes the payload through a `java.lang.ProcessBuilder` OGNL sink, base64-wrapping the command so arbitrary payload bytes survive OGNL string and URL encoding;
- supports a Unix Command target (`cmd/unix/*`) and a Linux Dropper target (CmdStager: curl / wget / printf / bourne).

AEM Forms on JEE only; AEM as a Cloud Service and AEM Sites (no `/adminui`) are not affected.

### Verification

- msftidy clean; module loads, `info` and targets render; `AutoCheck` prepended.
- The `/adminui/debug?expression=` endpoint and OGNL command-execution form match the public PoC and HackTricks writeup.
- On a licensed AEM Forms JEE 6.5.23.0 instance with Struts devMode enabled: `check` reports vulnerable via the concat probe; `run` returns a session. (No public containerised build exists for this product, so full session verification requires a licensed instance.)

Documentation is included under `documentation/modules/`.
79:T5339,# Async Meterpreter Mode

## Summary

Adds async mode for HTTP(S) Meterpreter sessions, allowing commands and Post modules to run across delayed, jittered check-ins and target-local work windows. Jobs execute under renewable leases, with status and output retained by request ID.

Also adds target-clock-aware wait estimates, async session indicators, safe timeout handling, and guardrails for incompatible transports, direct commands, and session lifecycle operations.

## Testing

### Automated

```sh
bundle exec rspec spec/lib/rex/post/meterpreter/async_result_store_spec.rb spec/lib/rex/post/meterpreter/async_window_spec.rb
```

### Manual

Using an HTTP(S) Meterpreter payload that supports the new async commands:

1. Obtain a session and run `async config -i 10 -j 0 -s 0 -e 24 -d all -l 30 -x 60`.
2. Run `async mode on`; confirm `sessions -l` marks the session as async.
3. Confirm direct commands such as `sysinfo` are rejected.
4. Run `async run sysinfo` and `async run run post/multi/gather/env SESSION=<id>`.
5. Use `async queue` and `async queue <rid>` to confirm status and captured output.
6. Run `async queue -c`, then `async mode off`; confirm normal interactive commands resume.
  

Payloads: https://github.com/rapid7/metasploit-payloads/pull/807

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Added asynchronous Meterpreter operation for HTTP(S) sessions, including configurable polling windows and target-time awareness.
  * Added commands to configure async mode, queue commands, view results, clear queued work, and manage leases.
  * Added status tracking for pending, running, completed, errored, and cancelled asynchronous tasks.
  * Added notifications when asynchronous commands complete and warnings for unavailable work windows.

* **Bug Fixes**
  * Improved response-timeout handling and shutdown behavior for asynchronous sessions.
  * Prevented unsupported session operations while async mode is enabled.
  * Ensured passive HTTP requests are acknowledged before processing their contents.

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->7a:Tab7,## Description

Adds an `auxiliary/scanner/http` module for **CVE-2026-54236** (GHSA-hgg8-fqqc-vfmw), the incomplete fix for CVE-2026-22778.

The CVE-2026-22778 fix added `sanitize_message` to the OpenAI router's exception handlers, so a malformed image no longer leaks Pillow's `BytesIO` object repr (`<_io.BytesIO object at 0x...>`, a live heap address). But the Anthropic-compatible router (`/v1/messages`, added early 2026) and the speech-to-text endpoints echo `str(exc)` directly, so the same malformed image still leaks the heap address verbatim through those paths. The leak weakens ASLR and is the entry primitive of the CVE-2026-22778 RCE chain. **All versions up to and including 0.23.0 are affected**; no fixed release was available at disclosure (fix pending in vllm-project/vllm#45119).

The module auto-detects the served model via `/v1/models` and sends a single benign malformed base64 image to `/v1/messages`. A leaked `0x` address → **Vulnerable**; a sanitized message or absent endpoint → **Safe**. The probe never reaches the downstream heap-overflow code path (CRASH_SAFE).

This complements the OpenAI-router scanner in #21592 (CVE-2026-22778); the two endpoints behave differently on a patched server, so a separate module is warranted.

## Verification

```
docker run -d -p 8000:8000 vllm/vllm-openai-cpu:latest-x86_64 \
  --model llava-hf/llava-interleave-qwen-0.5b-hf --max-model-len 4096 --enforce-eager
```

- [ ] `use auxiliary/scanner/http/vllm_anthropic_info_leak`
- [ ] `set RHOSTS <target>`
- [ ] `run`
- [ ] Expect on an affected server: `The target is vulnerable. Heap-address leak confirmed via Anthropic /v1/messages (vLLM <version>)`

Verified live against vLLM **0.23.0** (the latest release, which still leaks via `/v1/messages` while its OpenAI path is sanitized): module reports **Vulnerable** with the leaked heap address.
